# D04-store

The records: `internal/store`, which owns candidates' tables in its SQLite database, `state/candidates.db`, and every read and write of them (decision 16): `candidates`, one row per candidate business, never deleted (decision 4); `links`, one row per scrapes row attached to a candidate, addressed by its source key (decision 6); `estimates`, one per source per candidate; `judgments`, one per judged candidate, holding its two judgments alone (decision 8, funnel rename decisions 12 and 13); `decisions`, the owner's rulings (decision 10); `passes`, one row per pass until the prune removes it (decision 11); `zip_drive`, the ZIP drive-time table, replaced whole at every start (decision 9); `candidate_cargo`, one row per cargo tag of each candidate, kept beside the candidate's cargo for the previous release, which reads it, though nothing in this one does (log 30); `prospect_lists`, one row per prospect list until it is deleted (cohorts decision 23, funnel rename decisions 4 and 5); `rejects`, one row per reject or unreject record, never deleted (cohorts decisions 11 and 23); `enrichments`, one row per enrichment result recorded on a candidate, never deleted (website decisions 20 and 21); `facts`, one row per active candidate holding a result, the projection of its newest result of each kind and of its newest `brief` result whose run is not failed, its website decided by a check that found a site, unless a confirmed search run against that site found another, else by a confirmed search, else `unknown` from `places` while a website Places reported has not yet been given to the check, else `none` by a search that found nothing for a company with no site on record, else `unknown`, its known-since date and newest filing with the source each came from, and its drive minutes by a route found from its current address and the origin or else by the ZIP table (enrichers decision 5, brief decisions 7 and 8, website search decision 7, drive route outcome 7, website states outcomes 1 and 2, empty sites decision 3, facts from the candidate); `pages`, one row per copy of a web page kept on a candidate, never deleted, readable or unreadable with its reason (site text decision 1, page text outcome 3); `page_refetches`, the pages' refetch work list, one row per page `0027` found stopped at the old read limit, each settled once with its outcome and never deleted (page text outcome 6); `campaign_uses`, one row per campaign's use of a candidate, kept on the candidate at the end of its merge chain (campaigns decision 17, campaigns log D1); `opens`, one row per recorded open of a prospect list, never deleted, not even with its prospect list (basis decisions 7, 10 and 12); `link_name_keys`, one row per name key a link holds, kept under the link's source key (form5500 decision 15); and, for the parks (`D18-parks`), `parks`, one row per park, never deleted, `park_parcels`, one row per parcel of each park, `park_rules`, the one row holding the rules' three numbers and the run the parks were last derived from, `park_labels`, one row per county label of the rules, and `park_counties`, one row per county the newest derivation read; `places`, one row per place a walk found under one walk, never deleted; `park_walks`, one row per walk of a park, never deleted (`D19-walk`). This document declares the store's whole public surface: its records, the words they are spelled with, every method with its signature, the errors every consumer tells apart and the one line every consumer answers when the database cannot be reached. It states what the pass records, the ZIP table, the reads and their windows, the prospect list, reject and open writes and reads, the enrichment writes and reads, the derived kinds' runs, the targeted walks, the facts rows, the page writes and reads, the campaign use writes and reads, the park writes and reads, the schema's eighth to twenty-third migrations, the prune and `db status` do, and how every walk over the space is worked a page at a time (Bounded work). What a row does to the candidates and links when it is attached, what a gone run does, and what each ruling does (`Attach`, `MarkGone`, `Decide`) are `D12-ingest`'s; what a judge writes, the fleet verdict and its counts included (`Judge`, `JudgeAll`), is `D13-judging`'s; what a prospect list is, which validation set each `ValidProspectList` function accepts, and which candidates a prospect list's members and its matching count are, in which order, with which standing rejects, what a candidate's basis is and what `BasisOf` and `KindsOf` answer, and what a read's by basis and its changes since the list's previous open are, are `D14-prospect-lists`'; which pass calls which write is `D06-passes`'; what each kind's inputs are, what a candidate's subject holds, its pages, facts and website check among it, what each kind finds, what makes its found valid and when a prompts kind's result is failed are `D16-enrichers`'; what a website check finds and how a pass uses `DueEnrichments`, `EnrichmentMark` and `EnrichmentAfter` to fetch each inputs once are `D15-website-check`'s; which derived kinds a merge or a split re-runs, and that a merge moves the other candidate's pages to the survivor and a split leaves them, are `D12-ingest`'s; which targets an `enrich_target` walks, what a `site_text`, a `brief` or a `website_search` run records and how its counts are summed are `D06-passes`' and `D16-enrichers`'; which URL `add_page` may keep, and what it fetched, are `D10-tools`' and `D15-website-check`'s; which delivered events record a campaign use and what the delivery answers are `D06-passes`'; how a merge combines two candidates' campaign uses is `D12-ingest`'s; and which candidates the campaign filter admits, by its `min_campaign_age_days` and the uses' effective times, is `D14-prospect-lists`'; and what a park is, which parcels are industrial, how they cluster and are matched to the parks held, a park's name, the view words, the seed rules and the terms the park reads answer by are `D18-parks`', and which pass derives the parks, and when, is `D06-passes`'. Opening, migrating, transactions and the failure seam are appkit's `db`; `D01-layout-and-run-seam` resolves the path and declares the root package's `Migrations()`. It is scrapes' store in shape, with a pass in place of a run and the candidates, their evidence, the rulings and the prospect lists beside it. Beside its tables it keeps, unused by this release's reads, the tables and columns the previous release reads, `cohorts`, `assessments`, `rejects.cohort`, `campaign_uses.cohort` and `passes.rescored`, which `0017_prospect_lists.sql` copies from and leaves in place for a rollback and a later release drops (expand then contract; `0018` and `0019` leave them).

Decisions cited by number are the decisions document that delivered candidates; "judgments decision N" is decision N of the later one, *two judgments and the PPP reader*, which splits each candidate's one ranking number into two judgments and splits storing rows from judging them; "cohorts decision N" is decision N of the next, the cohorts change, which replaces the one ranked list with the owner's saved searches, now prospect lists, and "log N" is entry N of its decisions log; "website decision N" is decision N of the next, *the website check*, which checks each candidate's own domain for a working website, and "website log X" is entry X of its decisions log; "enrichers decision N" is decision N of the next, *candidates enrichers*, which makes every fact an enrichment, derived or fetched, kept in a facts row, and "enrichers settled N" is item N of its settled answers; "site text decision N" is decision N of the next, *candidates — site text*, which adds the `site_text` kind, the pages kept on a candidate, the targeted enrich and its pass kind, and one rule for which passes run together, and "settled for this delivery" marks a choice its delivery made where that document left it open; "brief decision N" is decision N of the next, *candidates — the brief*, which adds the `brief` kind, the pages and facts on a candidate's subject and the facts rows' `brief` and `brief_at`, and "P N" is item N of the decisions log of its delivery; "category evidence decision N" is decision N of the next, *candidates — category evidence*, which lets a good brief with a category decide a candidate's categories alone and gives FMCSA's trucking category only to carriers with for-hire authority; "website search decision N" is decision N of the next, *candidates — website search*, which adds the `website_search` kind, the site it finds and confirms, and the facts rows' `website_source` and `website_search_at`, and "S N" is entry DN of the decisions log of its delivery; "campaigns decision N" is decision N of the latest, *campaigns*, which adds the campaigns app, whose events candidates consumes to keep each business's campaign history and filter prospect lists by it, and "campaigns log DN" is entry DN of the decisions log of its delivery. "Funnel rename decision N" is decision N of the decisions document of the funnel rename, which gives what candidates stores and answers the funnel's stage names: prospect lists, ingest, judgments. "Drive route outcome N" is outcome N of *candidates — exact drive time*, which adds the `drive_route` kind, the drive a routing service finds from the base to a company's street address, and the facts rows' `minutes_source`, `miles`, `route_address` and `drive_route_at`. "Basis decision N" is outcome N of the decisions document of the prospect basis change, which gives every prospect list member a basis and every open the changes since the previous one. "Website states outcome N" is outcome N of *candidates — website states*, which makes a company's website found by the check or the search, `none` when the website search ran and found nothing, or `unknown` when no search has run, so a check's failed fetch no longer decides `none`. "Revenue bands decision N" is decision N of *candidates — PPP jobs and the revenue bands*, which makes the revenue estimate headcount times a band for the kind of business: the facts rows gain the headcounts the links gave and the newest good brief's labels, the judgments gain the headcount a judge picked, and a judge writes a `headcount` estimate of its own beside the sources'. "OSHA ITA outcome N" is outcome N of the reader in the decisions document of the OSHA ITA change, *the OSHA 300A summaries scraper and reader*, which adds the `osha_ita` source, reading each establishment's yearly OSHA Form 300A summary as an `employees` headcount with the hours worked beside it and keeping the establishment and its figures on each link, and "OSHA ITA P N" is entry P N of the decisions log of its delivery. "Headcount trend decision N" is outcome N of *candidates — the headcount series and trend*, under which a judge writes, from the yearly headcounts the facts row already holds, a headcount series, one figure per kind per year, and a trend, growing, flat, shrinking or unknown, with the kind, the two years and the two figures behind it, and which makes the trend a prospect list filter and order field. "Empty sites outcome N" is outcome N of *candidates — the website search after an empty site*, which runs the website search also for a company whose site on record site text kept no pages from, records on each search result why it ran, the site it ran against and which case its answer is, lets a different site it confirmed take over from the check of that site, and counts each pass's search results by case; "empty sites decision N" is decision N of its delivery.

## One database, one writer

candidates is the only writer of its database, and the database is the only record of its candidates, links, estimates, judgments, rulings, passes, prospect lists, reject records, enrichment results, facts rows, pages, the refetch work list, campaign uses, drive times, parks with their parcels, and the park rules with the run and counties of the newest derivation; nothing else is kept on disk (decision 1). `store.New` builds a store over a handle and opens or creates nothing. What a store holds when its handle is closed is what a store over the next `db.Open` of the same path holds; a test proves the records that way, never by reading tables. A store whose handle is failing (`SetFailing(true)`) answers every call with `ErrUnreachable` and changes nothing. `Config.Now` is the clock every stamp is read from, the moment a judge measures freshness at, and the clock a prospect list read measures filing age by (log 3); `Config.Rand` is the source pass ids, candidate ids, prospect list ids and park ids are drawn from. `Config.Origin` is the address the `drive_route` kind measures the drive from, which the store puts on every subject it builds, so it is part of that kind's inputs and of which route decides a facts row (drive route outcomes 2 and 3). `Config.Sleep` is the pause a busy write unit waits before the store writes it again (Busy writes, below). `Run` hands in `Process.Now`, `Process.Rand`, the origin setting and a pause: `Process.Sleep`, or, when it is nil, a pause of real time that ends early when its context is done, as `Process` reads a nil `Sleep` (`D01-layout-and-run-seam`); and the store has no default for any of them, a nil `Sleep` meaning no busy write is written again: an empty `Origin` gives every candidate empty `drive_route` inputs, so none is due and no route decides.

## By key

A pass costs in proportion to its rows, and the pages and the tools answer while it runs (orchestrator ruling, after a pass over the real FMCSA run of 16,706 rows never finished: every batch loaded, copied and compared the whole database under one lock). The store reaches every record the pass path needs by key through SQL, on decision 16's indexes and `0021_link_keys.sql`'s: a link by its source key (the links' unique scraper and source id), the active candidates one of whose links holds an EIN key by the links' EIN key, and those one of whose links holds a name key with a state and ZIP by the link name keys' name key, joined to the links by their source key, and by the links' name key, state and ZIP, the oldest first, a candidate's links by the links' candidate, a candidate by its id, its estimates, judgments and rulings by its id, a ZIP's minutes by the ZIP. No write on the pass path reads a whole table: `JudgeAll`, which a judge pass and a full ingest walk over every active candidate, judges one page of the active candidates per call, in id order after the caller's cursor (Bounded work, below). An `Attach` touches its batch's rows and the candidates they attach to: an unchanged row costs the lookup of its link and the write of its run, last seen and gone, and so does a row of a forced `Attach` whose newly computed reading equals the one its link holds, which rewrites neither the link's reading nor its key rows (`D12-ingest`); a candidate's display is re-derived from its own links once per batch for each candidate one of whose links the batch wrote, since a link's gone and reading order the display and a re-read can clear the one and change the other (`D12-ingest`); its last seen orders nothing. `MarkGone` examines one page of its scraper's links per call, in source id order after the caller's cursor, marks gone those last seen in a run other than the call's run, and re-derives by key the display of each candidate holding a link it marked, since gone orders the display, bounded by those candidates. `Judge` reads and writes each candidate it is given by its id, reading the candidate's record, its own links, rulings and facts row, and writing its estimates and judgments. An ingest judges nothing, so its cost is its rows alone (judgments decision 23). A ruling works the same way, touching only the candidates it names, each by key (its record, its own links, its rulings, its results and facts row, and the ZIP table's minutes for its display ZIP), so its cost is bounded by those candidates' links: a `not_same` judges none and re-derives nothing; an `exclude` or `include` judges `r.Candidate`; a merge or a split re-derives the display of, re-runs the derived kinds for, and judges the candidates it touches (`r.Candidate` and `r.Other`, or `r.Candidate` and the split's new candidate). It reads no table whole and refreshes no other candidate (`D12-ingest`). A prospect list write touches its one prospect list row, and a reject or unreject its one pair's records, its prospect list and its candidate, each by key. An enrichment write touches the candidates its results name, each by key, and rewrites their facts rows from their newest results; a `Derive` reads one window of the active candidates in id order and, for each, its subject by key (its links, its ZIP's minutes) and its newest result of the kind; and a read of a candidate's results, of the results of one inputs after a mark, or of the due candidates of one page of the active candidates in id order reads by key and by window on `0011`'s and `0012`'s indexes and the candidates' id (`D06-passes` and `D15-website-check` bound the pass's memory). `FillFacts` walks the active candidates once each start, in the background after `READY=1` (the engine's `StartFill`, `D06-passes`), one page per call, writing a row only where one is missing, where the candidate holds a `drive_route` result, a set no larger than the prospects the owner has routed, or where the row's minutes and source disagree, which only a rollback leaves. The upkeep the start and the sweep run touches only its own table (log 36, after candidates was killed at its start on the `us` space under `memory_max = 256M`, the two upkeep writes loading, copying and comparing every candidate, link, estimate, judgments and ruling of a database holding 43,000 candidates and 54,000 links): `ReplaceZipDrive` reads and writes `zip_drive` alone, and `PrunePasses` reads and deletes in `passes` alone, at most the `n` passes it is given per call, so the memory either takes does not grow with the candidates, links, estimates, judgments or rulings the store holds. A page write touches its one candidate's pages, by key on `0013`'s index on the candidate and the URL, which is also how the cap is counted inside the write; `Pages` and `CountPages` read one candidate's pages on that index and `GetPage` one page by its number; and `DueAmong`, `DeriveAmong` and `Subject` read and write only the candidates whose ids they are given, each by key, so a targeted pass costs in proportion to its targets. A subject built for a `Prompter` kind also reads its candidate's pages, their text included, by key on `0013`'s index, and its newest website check whose inputs share a URL with its current inputs, its Places URL and its email's domain, by key on `0011`'s index on the candidate and the kind; no other subject reads them, so no untargeted walk reads a page (P1). No call of the store loads every record.

Every write is one write transaction of appkit's `db`, and the store holds no lock of its own across one: the only lock it keeps guards `Config.Rand`. A read goes through `db.Read`, which answers from what was last committed while a write transaction is open, since appkit's handle reads beside its one writer; so a page or a tool never waits for a batch, a page, a ruling or a prospect list write. Calls racing still behave as one after another (`D12-ingest`, R-W2NM-7T0P, R-O9H7-R42Y), because every write goes through appkit's one writer. Which statements the store issues is not contract; that a read answers while a write is open is, and so is the time a pass over thousands of rows takes (`D06-passes`).

## Busy writes

A write can find the database's write lock held by another connection past the busy timeout appkit's `db.Open` sets, as litestream's checkpoint of a large WAL holds it on a host; the driver then answers `SQLITE_BUSY`, which the store reads as the low eight bits of the result code the driver error's exported `Code() int` gives through `errors.As` against an interface, so candidates imports no driver (scrapes' store reads a lock the same way). These writes are retried, the **write units**: the write of an `Attach`, `MarkGone`, `Derive`, `JudgeAll`, `FillFacts`, `Rebuild`, `PrunePasses` or `ApplyParks` call (`Rebuild`, the full ingest's page of candidates rebuilt by the orders, `D12-ingest`, joins them as a page of `FillFacts`' shape), each one store call, a batch or a page, whose transaction the store owns, and each idempotent, so writing it again is safe. A busy unit is written again after a pause through `Config.Sleep`, `store.WriteBackoff` doubling, 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 seconds, for up to `store.WriteRetries` retries; a unit still busy after them, or refused for any other reason, ends the call with an error matching `ErrUnreachable`, as before, and a pause that ends with the call's context done ends it with one matching both `ErrUnreachable` and the context's error. A failed write changes nothing, so a unit retried and then written leaves the store as one undisturbed call would. With a nil `Sleep` nothing is retried and a busy unit fails at once, as every write did before. No other method retries: P15 named the batch, the derive and the judge, the writes a full ingest makes by the thousand, and P17 made `MarkGone`, `FillFacts` and `PrunePasses` pages of the same shape, walked by the hundred on a large space; every other write, a ruling's, a pass record's, a prospect list's or an enrichment's, touches a few rows by key and answers a busy database `Unreachable` as before.

## The records

A **candidate** (`store.Candidate`) is one business: its id, `cand_` and 16 lowercase hexadecimal digits; its status, `active` or `merged`, with the id it was merged into; its display fields (name, every name, street, city, state, ZIP, county as `MN-115`, phone, email, people); its tags (class and cargo); and when it was created and last updated (decision 4). Beside the display it keeps where each displayed fact came from (the facts-from-the-candidate change): the source identifier and the record key (`sources.SourceKey`) of its name, of its address, one pair for the street, city, state and ZIP together, of its phone and of its email; each person with the source and record it was taken from; and every other value its records hold for the name, the address, the phone and the email, each a `sources.Sourced` with its own source and record, as `sources.Pick` answers them (`D05-sources`). Which values the display takes, by the orders, and when they change is `D12-ingest`'s; the store keeps them and answers them with the candidate, so the card, the tools and the enrichers read them from `GetCandidate` and never pick again. `names` stays the union of every name its links use, display name first, as the previous release reads it and `ListCandidates`' name filter matches it.

A **link** (`store.Link`) is one row's attachment to one candidate: the candidate, the source key (scraper and source id), the run the row was last seen in and its digest, the source's reading of the row as last seen (`sources.Reading`: the keys, the envelope's common fields, the tags, the filing date, the fleet test and the row's fleet verdict and counts, the estimate, the operating evidence, the date the row shows the business existing since, the category, the government name match, the headcount with the hours worked beside its count, and the establishment name and the filing year, annual average employees and hours worked as the row gave them, which only an OSHA ITA row sets; OSHA ITA outcome 16), how it attached (`source_key`, `ein`, `match_key`, `decision`, or, for a place a walk found, `phone` or `domain`) with its confidence, when it was first and last seen, and whether it is gone (decision 6). A source key is unique among links: a row attaches to one candidate at a time. Beside the reading, the store keeps the keys a link **holds** where it can find them by key (form5500 decision 15): the reading's `EINKey` in the link's `ein_key`, empty for none, and each element of its `NameKeys`, in order, as a row of `link_name_keys` under the link's source key, while `name_key` keeps its `NameKey` as before; so a link holds its `NameKey` and the elements of its `NameKeys`, and its `EINKey` when that is not empty. Which candidate a row finds by those keys is `D12-ingest`'s. A link a walk made (`places.Name`) also carries the newest walk that found its place and that walk's park; it has no run or digest and is never marked gone, and it joins a candidate's display only when the candidate has no other link (`D19-walk`, `D12-ingest`).

An **estimate** is one source's `sources.Estimate` for one candidate, at most one per source per candidate; beside the sources' a judge writes one of its own, under the source word `judging.HeadcountSource`, `headcount`, formed from the facts row's headcounts and brief labels and the candidate's categories (revenue bands decision 8, `D13-judging`), which the estimates table holds and `Estimates` answers as any other, in source order (R-5AWM-GOIZ). A candidate's **judgments** (`store.Judgments`) are its two judgments as the last judge wrote them (judgments decision 1, funnel rename decisions 12 and 13): its **operating confidence**, how sure we are that it is a business operating today, with the **operating evidence** behind it, and its **revenue estimate**, a low, a high and a confidence, with the **headcount** it was formed from, a low and a high, equal unless the headcounts disagree, and its basis, the kinds and scrapers that gave it (revenue bands decisions 6 and 7); its **headcount series**, one figure per kind per year from the facts row's dated `employees`, `participants` and `jobs` headcounts, a gap where a year has none, with the scraper behind each kind's newest figure, and its **trend**, `growing`, `flat`, `shrinking` or `unknown`, with its basis, the kind it was read in, the comparison year and the newest year, their two figures and the change between them (headcount trend decisions 1 to 9); beside them whether the owner's standing ruling excludes it, the rules version and when they were calculated. They hold no fact: the facts the prospect list filters read (`D14-prospect-lists`) are its facts row's, below, read at every read: the date the business is known since, the government name pattern its name matched, the newest filing among its links not gone, its categories each with its source, the **fleet verdict**, `pass`, `fail` or `unknown`, with the power units and drivers of the FMCSA link it was read from (cohorts decision 13, log 20), the **headcounts** its links gave, each with its scraper, kind, count and date, and the **brief labels**, those of its newest good brief (revenue bands decisions 4 and 5), the **website** state, `https`, `http`, `none` when the website search ran and found nothing, or `unknown` when nothing decided it (website states outcome 1), with the URL the check or the search landed on, empty unless `https` or `http` (website decision 14), and which of the two decided it, `check`, `search` or empty for neither (website search decisions 7 and 10), and the drive minutes, with which result decided them, `route` or `zip`, the route's miles and the address the route was found to (drive route outcome 7). A prospect list's member (`store.Ranked`) carries both, its judgments and its facts, the facts at their unknown values when the candidate has no facts row. The verdict replaces the boolean fleet test in the Go type, the tools and the pages under one name, `fleet_verdict` (log 10); the `fleet` column the store wrote beside it for an earlier release is dropped by `0011` (website decision 22). The judgments are stored in `judgments`, which `0017` fills from the previous release's `assessments` with the judgment columns only, `scored` becoming `calculated`; a judge writes `judgments` and `estimates` and never `assessments`, which the previous release keeps reading and which goes stale after the upgrade (Known limits). Its **operating key** is the operating confidence scaled by a million and rounded to an integer, -1 when it has none: what a prospect list's order compares operating confidence by (`D14-prospect-lists`, `D13-judging`). A judgment the evidence does not support is empty, never 0 (judgments decision 2). What a judge computes is `D13-judging`'s; judgments are written only by a judge: a judge pass, a full ingest, or a ruling re-judging the candidates it touches and no other (`JudgeAll`, `Judge`, `Decide`). An ingest of one run or one scraper stores links and writes no judgment. A read shows what was last written and computes nothing, so a store read between two judges shows each judgment as the last judge wrote it, and one during a judge may show some candidates judged anew and others as the judge before left them (Known limits) and each fact as the last enrichment wrote it; nothing decays between writes (judgments decision 6). `GetJudgments` answers a merged candidate's last judgments too, since a merge leaves them; a merged candidate has no facts row.

A **ruling** (`store.Ruling`) is one of the owner's decisions: its kind (`merge`, `split`, `not_same`, `exclude`, `include`), the candidate it names, the other candidate (the other side of a merge or a `not_same`, the new candidate of a split), the source keys a split moved, why, who (user id and email) and when (decision 10). Rulings are never changed or removed. The `exclude` and `include` tools and `decide` all record theirs through `Decide`, unchanged (cohorts decision 12).

A **prospect list** (`store.ProspectList`) is one saved search: its id, `pl_` and 16 lowercase hexadecimal digits; its name, free text, not unique; its size; its filters (`store.ProspectListFilters`); its order (`[]store.ProspectListOrder`); who created it (user id and email) and when; and who last changed it and when, which creation sets to the creator and the creation time (cohorts decision 1, log 24). Its members are never stored: `ReadProspectList` works them out at every read (cohorts decisions 3 and 25). The store keeps the filters and the order in their **normal form**, the categories de-duplicated and in byte order, the basis words de-duplicated and in the order records, partial, enriched, and an empty order as the default order, so an edit that changes nothing compares equal and writes nothing (log 8, log 16). An edit (`store.ProspectListEdit`) names the prospect list and any of its name, size, filters and order, nil for each part it leaves alone; a passed filter set or order replaces the stored one whole, so `&ProspectListFilters{}` clears every filter and `&[]ProspectListOrder{}` sets the default order (cohorts decision 6, log 8). A delete removes the row; its reject records and its opens stay.

A **reject record** (`store.Reject`) is one reject or unreject: its kind (`reject` or `unreject`), the prospect list and the candidate it names, why, who and when (cohorts decision 10). Records are never changed or removed, not at an unreject and not when their prospect list is deleted (cohorts decision 11). Which record stands for a prospect list and a candidate is `D14-prospect-lists`' **standing reject**. `ProspectListName` is filled on every read from the prospect list the record names while that prospect list exists, so it is empty once the prospect list is deleted.

An **open** is one recorded open of a prospect list (basis decision 7): a row of `opens` holding the prospect list's id, when it was opened, who opened it (user id and email), the definition it was opened under (its filters, order and size, in normal form), the members it answered, in rank order, each with its basis then, and its **passing** set, the ids of the matching candidates in rank order before the size cut, so a member that joined can be told as having moved up from below the cut or as newly passing (basis decision 9). It is a `store.Open`, each member a `store.OpenMember`, and `Opens` answers a list's opens newest first, a deleted list's included, as `Rejects` answers a candidate's reject records; `ReadProspectList` and `OpenProspectList` read a list's newest open to answer its changes (`D14-prospect-lists`' previous open). No tool and no page exposes `Opens`: it is the store's record, read by the tests, and who opened is answered nowhere else. The row keeps `at` as RFC 3339 UTC to the second and the filters, order, members and passing set as JSON, as the other tables keep theirs; no other release reads them. Opens are never changed or removed, not when their prospect list is deleted, and never pruned (basis decisions 10 and 12). A **basis** is never stored: it is `BasisOf` the candidate's results, worked out at every read (basis decision 1), and an open keeps only the basis each member had then.

An **enrichment result** (`store.Enrichment`) is one thing found about a candidate, from its own links, from the ZIP table or from outside (website decision 20, enrichers decisions 1 and 4): the candidate it is on; its kind, one of `enrichers.Kinds()`, `drive_minutes`, `link_facts`, `website_check`, `website_search`, `site_text`, `brief` and `drive_route` (`D16-enrichers`); its **inputs**, what it was found from, a ZIP, a fingerprint of the candidate's links, a domain, a fingerprint of the candidate's name, city and state, a website URL, a fingerprint of the candidate's pages or a fingerprint of its street, city, state, ZIP and the origin; its source word (`zip_drive`, `links`, the domain checked, `prompts` or `google_routes`); what was found, as the kind's JSON object; what it cost and when it was recorded. Results are history (website decision 21): never changed but by a merge, which moves the other candidate's results to the survivor, and never removed; a redo adds a result, and a candidate's **newest result** of a kind is the one recorded last, as `Enrichments` orders them, not the one with the latest time, so a result moved by a merge keeps its place. A candidate is **due** for a kind when it is active, its inputs for the kind now are not empty, and it has no result of the kind or its newest one was found from other inputs (enrichers decision 3): no age makes a result due.

A **facts row** (`store.Facts`) is the projection of a candidate's newest result of each kind (enrichers decision 5): the link facts from its newest `link_facts` result, the website state and URL and which result decided them from its website decision, below, the drive minutes from its drive decision, below, each kind's facts at their unknown values when it has none, and, per kind written, when the result it came from was recorded; `site_text` gives no fact of its own, only that time (site text decision 11), which a prospect list's member answers as when site text was last written for it (`D14-prospect-lists`). `brief` gives the row's `brief`, the summary of its newest result whose run is not failed, its **newest good brief**, and, when that result gave at least one category, the row's categories: that result's alone, each with the source word `brief`, in place of the link facts' (category evidence decisions 1 and 2); a good brief with no category leaves the link facts' categories standing, and the two sets are never mixed; the newest good brief also gives the row's `brief_labels`, its labels, as `link_facts` gives the row's `headcounts` (revenue bands decisions 4 and 5, R-EH1E-KVAS), each with the hours worked beside its count when its row gave them and one per link, so the sites of one business in one year stay one entry each, never summed here; summing is the judge's (OSHA ITA outcomes 11, 12 and 16, OSHA ITA P6.3, R-H6LD-6IMC); its time, like every kind's, is that of its newest result, failed or not, which a prospect list's member answers as when the brief was last written for it (brief decisions 6 to 8, `D14-prospect-lists`). The **website decision** of a candidate's results, read newest first, has a source, a state, a URL, a title, a why and a time (R-QJ7S-Y7FR): when its newest `website_check` result found a site, `https` or `http`, that result decides, with the source `check`, unless a **takeover search** stands against it: a `website_search` result, recorded before or after it, whose run is not failed, whose confirmation loaded a page on a host other than that of its recorded website, and whose recorded website is on the host of the URL that check found, hosts compared as the site key compares them, lowercased with one trailing dot and one leading `www.` removed, or is a site a search had decided, its `website_from` `search`, whatever case it recorded (empty sites decisions 3, 17 and 19); otherwise its newest `website_search` result whose run is not failed, whose confirmation loaded a page and that is not inert, its **confirmed search**, decides, with the source `search`, the confirmation's state, URL and title and the agent's why; otherwise its newest `website_search` result whose run is not failed, that is not inert and that named no URL, or named one whose confirmation found `none`, its **unfound search**, decides `none`, with no source, URL or title, the agent's why and its own time; otherwise nothing decides, the state is `unknown`, the source, URL and why are empty, and the title and time are the newest check's, or empty and zero with none (website search decision 7, website states outcomes 1 to 3). So `none` means a search ran and found nothing, and `unknown` that no site has been found and no search has come back empty: a check's `none`, a domain that answers nothing, is still stored as a result but decides nothing, so a candidate the check alone found nothing for reads `unknown` (website states outcome 2). A search is **inert** when it ran against a website on record and its confirmation loaded a page on that site's host, or, holding no confirmation, it answered that site back, case `stands`, or answered no site, case `none`, or it named a site that did not load: it decides nothing, so the decision is what it would be without it, a found site never turning `none` (empty sites outcomes 6 to 8, empty sites decisions 3 and 17). A search recorded before its cases were records no website and no case, so it is never inert and never a takeover search, and decides as it did. Only the newest check is read, so an older check that found a site does not decide behind a newer one that found nothing. A later check that finds a site takes over from either search, but a check that finds a URL on the host a takeover search ran against does not take it back, whatever their order, and no check takes back a takeover by a search that ran against a site a search had decided; a later check that fails or finds nothing leaves either standing, and nothing in the store ages a `none`, which only a forced search redoes (website states outcome 4, `D16-enrichers`); a newer search that finds nothing, fails or names a site that does not load leaves an older confirmed site standing; a failed search, or one that named a URL and holds no confirmation, leaves an older unfound search deciding; and a full ingest of the row from the results gives the same answer. Between the check and an unfound search stands a website a source reported (facts from the candidate): the candidate's **Places URL** (R-RYYW-L4WX), the website the newest walk that found one of its places recorded for it, trimmed, the first in the order `Links` answers its places links; while no `website_check` result of the candidate was given that URL, that is no result whose inputs, split by `website.URLs`, hold it, the state is `unknown`, the source `places` and the URL empty, so a candidate the walk brought in reads as holding a site still to be checked rather than none, and once the check has been given the URL the decision is what the check, the searches and nothing else make it. A check that found nothing on every URL it was given decides nothing, as a check of a domain alone never did. Ahead of everything the owner's entry would decide, with the source `owner`, but candidates holds no website the owner entered and nothing fills that slot today, so no requirement names it beyond its word, `store.WebsiteByOwner`. The decision also has a **checked URL**, the element of the check's inputs its winning attempts began from, the result's `From` as recorded: the reported URL or the bare domain, and empty when the check does not decide or its result, recorded before the check took a reported website, has no `From`; nothing synthesizes one. The row keeps the decision's state, URL, source and checked URL, the source as `store.WebsiteByCheck`, `store.WebsiteBySearch` or `store.WebsiteByPlaces`, and `website_search` adds its time as every kind does; the candidate page reads all seven parts from the results (`D08-pages`). The Places URL is only a URL the check would be offered, `website.Inputs`' offered URL (`D15-website-check`): a website Places reported that is no absolute `http` or `https` URL with a host, or whose host is an IP address, is passed over, since the check would never be given it and it would otherwise read `places` for ever. `PlacesURL` answers it, the one read of it the pages, the rebuild and the enrichers' subject use. Since it is read from the candidate's links and place records, every write that changes it, a walk's `RecordSweep` or a merge or split moving a places link, writes the candidate's facts row anew in the same write (R-D6P8-1L4E), so `GetFacts` and the card never disagree, and a candidate a walk founds holds a facts row from that write, reading `places` before any result is recorded on it, which a prospect list's member reads (R-D31I-W9WB). The known-since date and the newest filing carry the source each came from, as the newest `link_facts` result found them by the orders (`D05-sources`): `enrichers.LinkFound` gains `KnownSinceSource` and `NewestFilingSource`, JSON members `known_since_source` and `newest_filing_source`, each absent when empty, additions `D16-enrichers` declares (R-AQ5I-5R6H, R-ZV8W-PNJN), so a `link_facts` result recorded before them reads both empty. It is written in the same write as the result that changes it, so it is never behind its results, and it can be rebuilt from them at any time; it is what the prospect lists, the tools and the pages read a candidate's facts from. The **drive decision** (R-EFTI-73K3) has minutes, a source, miles and a route address: the newest `drive_route` result whose inputs equal the candidate's `drive_route` inputs now, its record-chosen address, the address its records choose with the route slot skipped, with the store's origin (R-B3KE-D8C4 and R-DCSP-YFTV in `D16-enrichers`, R-2BTS-XFVR in `D12-ingest`), that is not failed and found minutes decides, with the source `route`, its miles and the address the service resolved; otherwise the newest `drive_minutes` result gives the minutes, with the source `zip` when it found some and empty when it found none, and no miles or route address (drive route outcome 7). So a newer ZIP result never displaces a matching route (outcome 9), a route found from an older address or another origin stops deciding at the next write of the row, and a failed or unroutable route leaves an older matching route deciding, as a failed brief leaves the last good one. The words are `store.MinutesByRoute` and `store.MinutesByZip`, as the facts row's `minutes_source` stores them; `drive_route` adds its time as every kind does. Beside the row, `GetFacts` answers the candidate's **records**, the link facts' categories with their sources, read at the read from its newest `link_facts` result and kept in no column, so the categories the records give stay readable when a brief decided (category evidence decision 4).

Which set decided is read from the set, never stored (category evidence decisions 3 and 11): `store.ClassifiedBy` of a set of categories is `brief` when every one carries the source `brief`, empty when there is none, and `links` otherwise, and `store.RecordsDiffer` of the categories and the records is true when the brief decided and the records name other categories than it. Both are pure functions over what the facts row and the records hold, so the `candidate` tool and the candidate page derive the same answer from one `GetFacts`, and a prospect list member's classification is `ClassifiedBy` of its facts' categories, the same categories the category filter reads, so the filter and the label never disagree.

A **page** (`store.Page`) is one copy of a web page kept on a candidate (site text decision 1): the candidate it is on, its **number**, the URL it was fetched from, its title, its text, the bytes of its body read, when it was fetched, and who added it, by user id and email. The store mints the number, which names the copy among every page the store holds, not only the candidate's, so a merge that moves a page keeps its number and its text page's path, `/candidates/<id>/pages/<n>`, names it on the survivor (`D08-pages`, `D12-ingest`). Adding a URL a candidate already holds keeps a newer copy beside the old; the newest copy of each URL, the one with the highest number, is the one read. Pages are never changed but by a merge and never removed.

A **campaign use** (`store.CampaignUse`) is one campaign's use of one candidate, as campaigns' events told it (campaigns decisions 16 and 17): the candidate it is recorded under, the campaign's id, the prospect list the candidate was added from, empty when it was added one at a time, when it was added, when it was rejected and why, and when a piece was last delivered to it. A zero time is one not known: a rejection or a delivery may be told before the add it follows, since the bus retries and does not order. A candidate holds at most one use of a campaign, since a business is in a campaign once (campaigns decision 3). A use's **effective time** is when it was added, or, while no add is known, the earlier of its rejection and its last delivery: the moment the prospect list filter measures a campaign's age from (`D14-prospect-lists`, campaigns decision 18, where used means added). A rejected use is still a use; that a reject is final inside the campaign is campaigns' (campaigns decision 5, campaigns log D4), and it touches no prospect list reject record here.

A **park** (`store.Park`) is one industrial park derived from the parcel records (`D18-parks`): its id, `park_` and 16 lowercase hexadecimal digits; its county, town and number, fixed when it is minted; its name, read from its town and the number of parks of its county and town, never stored; its state, `active` or `gone`, a gone park never deleted and kept with its parcels so that it can come back; its center, radius, ZIP, parcel count and building value, as the derivation that last created or updated it gave them, and that derivation's run; its drive minutes, read from the ZIP table at every read, so a new table moves them with no derivation; the count it grew from; its newest walk's date, pass id (`WalkRun`), places found, new candidates and whether the ceiling cut it, read from its walks (`D19-walk`); and when it was minted and last applied. A **park parcel** (`store.ParkParcel`) is one parcel of a park as the derivation read it: its PIN, owners, site address, building value, the rule that picked it and its point. The **park rules** are one row of three numbers and the walk ceiling and the **label rows**, each a county and a label, kept in the order an edit gave them; beside them the store keeps the run and the counties of the newest derivation it applied, the run the start compares the current run against (`D06-passes`) and the counties the parks page lists. None of these is a candidate's record, and the derivation ties no park to a candidate (parks outcome 9); a candidate is found in a park only through a walk, whose places link carries the park (`D19-walk`).

A **pass** (`store.Pass`) is one ingest, one judge, one enrich, one enrich over a target, one derivation of the parks or one walk. Its id is `pass_` and 16 lowercase hexadecimal digits. It keeps its **kind**, what it does: `ingest` stores one run's rows as links and marks the vanished ones gone, or, started by the `ingest` tool, does so for one scraper's current run or, named no scraper, for every scraper's current run and then re-runs every derived kind for every active candidate and judges (a **full ingest**; funnel rename decision 9), `judge` judges every active candidate, `enrich` records what the enrichers find for the candidates due, derived kinds before fetched ones, and `enrich_target` runs one kind for one prospect list's members or one candidate, the due ones or, forced, all of them (`D06-passes`; site text decisions 12 to 14), and `parks` derives the parks from one `mn_parcels` run (`D18-parks`) and `walk` searches the parks a `walk` call named with Google Places (`D19-walk`); its **origin**, what started it: `run`, a `scrape.finished` delivery, `cron`, a delivery of the daily trigger's event, `ingest`, the `ingest` tool, `judge`, the `judge` tool, `enrich`, the `enrich` tool, for either enrich kind, `pass`, another pass (website log A1), `rules`, an `edit_park_rules` call, or `start`, the start's read of the current `mn_parcels` run, or `walk`, the `walk` tool, a `run` delivery starting a parks pass for a `scrape.finished` of `mn_parcels` as it starts an ingest for a registered scraper's; its **trigger**, the run id an ingest's or a parks pass's delivery named or, for a parks pass the start started, the run it read, the trigger's slug `judge_candidates` for a judge the bus started, `ingest` or `judge` for a pass the tool of that name started, `enrich` for an enrich or an `enrich_target` the tool started, `edit_park_rules` for a parks pass that tool started, `walk` for a walk, or the id of the pass that started it (judgments decision 25, website log A1); for an `enrich_target` its **target**, the prospect list or candidate id it walks, and its **enrich kind**, the kind it walks, both empty for every other pass; for an ingest the `ingest` tool started its **scraper**, the one scraper it ingests, empty for a full ingest and every other pass; for a parks pass its **parcel run**, the run it derived the parks from, set when it ends done; its **status**, one of `running`, `done` and `failed`, and when failed one of five **reasons**, `scrapes`, `interrupted`, `store`, `prompts` and `places` (which failure is which is `D06-passes`'; `prompts` settled for this delivery, S5); **started** and **finished**, each UTC to the second; the thirty-five counts and, for a walk, whether it stopped at the walk ceiling, each 0 until the pass is done, `cost` among them in nano-dollars; its starter's user id and email; the request id it was started under; and, for a delivery, the bus event's id, the **delivery**, an internal column no tool or page shows: it is how a redelivered event is told from a new one (`D06-passes`).

Every id the store mints, a pass's, a candidate's, a prospect list's or a park's, is its prefix and 8 bytes read from `Config.Rand`, written as 16 lowercase hexadecimal digits, drawn again when it is the id of a record of its kind the store holds; a prospect list id is drawn again also when a reject record names it, so a deleted prospect list's id is never reused while its records stand (cohorts decision 1); a park id is drawn again also when the same apply minted it for an earlier park.

## Passes

A pass has three writes. `AddPass` records it `running`, stamping started and minting the id. It is refused `ErrDelivered` when its delivery is one an existing pass carries, or when its origin is `run` and its trigger is the run id an existing pass of origin `run` names, whatever that pass's status; and otherwise `ErrBusy` while a pass that blocks it is `running`: one of the same kind over the same **scope**, the scope of an ingest a delivery started being its run, of one the `ingest` tool started its scraper, empty for a full ingest, which is the corpus, of an `enrich_target` its target, and of a judge, an enrich and a parks pass the corpus (site text decision 15). Every other pair runs together: two ingests of different runs, an ingest of one scraper beside a delivered ingest of a run of that scraper and beside a full ingest, a judge beside an ingest, a full ingest or an enrich beside an `enrich_target`, and two `enrich_target` passes over different targets, a candidate and a prospect list holding it among them; two over one target are busy whatever kinds they walk (settled for this delivery, S10). A parks pass runs beside a pass of every other kind, and two parks passes are busy whatever their origins. A walk runs beside a pass of every other kind, and two walks are busy. A walk ends `done` by `FinishWalk`, which records its counts and whether the ceiling stopped it, or `failed` by `FailWalk`, which records the counts it spent up to the failure, unlike every other failed pass (`D19-walk`, walk outcome 8). An ingest naming a run another ingest already names is refused `ErrDelivered` before its scope is looked at, so an ingest's scope refuses nothing an ingest delivery can send. A judge's trigger is the same slug every day, so the trigger refuses only run ids, and a redelivered trigger event is told by its delivery alone. An `mn_parcels` run never names an ingest, since `mn_parcels` is no source (`D18-parks`), so the run rule needs no kind; a parks pass the start recorded names its run in its trigger under the origin `start`, which `AddPass`'s run rule does not refuse; a later delivery for parks of that run is skipped before any pass is recorded, by the engine's check for a pass whose delivery is the event's or whose trigger is the run, whatever its kind, origin and status (`D06-passes`, R-DYSZ-X1WW). `FinishPass` ends a `running` pass `done` with the thirty-five counts its work gathered, and `ApplyParks` ends a parks pass `done` in the write that applies its derivation (Parks, below); `FailPass` ends a `running` pass `failed` with a reason and leaves everything the pass wrote in place, since every write is idempotent and the next pass or a full ingest repairs it (decision 11). Of two calls racing on the same pass one succeeds and the other is told it is no longer running (`ErrEnded`). Nothing moves a pass back. `SetParkRules` records a parks pass of origin `rules` as `AddPass` would, in the write that edits the rules, so an edit refused `ErrBusy` changes nothing.

The counts a pass finishes with are the sums of the `store.Counts` its writes answered: `Attach` answers `Rows`, `New`, `Changed`, `Unchanged`, `Created` and `Linked` for a batch; `MarkGone` answers `Gone`; `Judge` and `JudgeAll` answer `Judged` and `Unranged`, `JudgeAll`'s summed by the pass over its walk. So an ingest of a run or of one scraper finishes with `Judged` and `Unranged` 0 and a judge with only those two. `Derive` answers the results it recorded, which a pass sums as `Derived`. An enrich finishes with only `Derived`, `Due`, `Fetched`, `HTTPS`, `HTTP` and `None`, the last five counted by its work itself, since no store write answers them (`D06-passes`, `D15-website-check`); a full ingest with its ingest's and judge's counts and `Derived`; an `enrich_target` with the enrich counts of the kind it walked and, for a prompts kind, `Runs`, `RunsFailed`, `Pages` and `Cost`, which its work counts itself, every other pass leaving those four 0. A parks pass finishes with only the eight parks counts its derivation gives, parcels read, by label, by value, by candidate and parks created, updated, gone and grown (`D18-parks`), which its work counts and `ApplyParks` records. What each counts is the owning document's. A walk finishes with only `ParksWalked`, `Circles`, `Requests`, `Found`, `NewCandidates` and `Cost`, which its work sums from its sweeps (`D19-walk`). An `enrich_target` of `website_search` also finishes with `SearchDifferent`, `SearchStands` and `SearchNone`, the results it recorded of each case, which its work counts itself; every other pass leaves those three 0 (`D06-passes`, empty sites decision 5).

`GetPass` answers any pass, whoever started it. `ListPasses` is one window of the passes in pass order, newest first: those after a position, the started text and id of the last pass of the window before, at most `n`; `RunningPasses` every pass still `running`, which the start finishes `failed` with `interrupted` (`D06-passes`). `PrunePasses` removes, in one write, at most `n` of the passes **past keeping**, the oldest first, and answers how many: every `done` pass beyond the newest keep count of `done` passes and every `failed` pass beyond the newest keep count of `failed` passes; a `running` pass is neither counted nor removed (decision 16). The start and the sweep call it until it answers 0 (`D03-serve`, `D06-passes`). A pruned pass is gone from every read, its delivery and trigger with it. Candidates, links, estimates, judgments, rulings, prospect lists, reject records, enrichment results, pages and campaign uses are never pruned; the prune touches none of them. A judge replaces a candidate's estimates and judgments (`D13-judging`); a merge moves the merged candidate's links to the survivor, and its enrichment results and pages with them, deletes the merged candidate's facts row, and the merged candidate otherwise keeps what it had at the merge: its display fields, tags, estimates, last judgments and reject records (`D12-ingest`).

## The ZIP table

`ReplaceZipDrive` replaces the whole table with the map it is given in one write; the start hands it the root package's `ZipDrive()`, so the table is always the release's (decision 9). `ZipMinutes` answers one ZIP's minutes. `drive_minutes` reads the table for each candidate's display ZIP when it derives (`D16-enrichers`); a judge no longer reads it (`D13-judging`). A new table reaches the facts only through a full ingest, which forces every derived kind, since a ZIP that did not change leaves its candidate's `drive_minutes` inputs unchanged.

## Prospect lists

`CreateProspectList` mints the id, stamps created and changed with the caller and the clock, and keeps the normal form of what it was given. `EditProspectList` replaces each part the edit passes, and when the prospect list that results is the one stored it writes nothing and keeps changed and its user as they were, so an empty edit or a repeated one is the same as none (log 8). `DeleteProspectList` removes the prospect list and answers it as it was; deleting it again is `ErrNotFound` (cohorts decision 7). `GetProspectList` answers a definition without its members. Each write refuses a part a `ValidProspectList` function refuses with `ErrInvalid` before it looks for the prospect list, and an empty user id `ErrUnreachable`, as no consumer sends one (log 7). Creating twice makes two prospect lists, as cohorts decision 18 accepts.

`ReadProspectList` answers the definition, its **matching** count, its **classified** count, how many of its members the brief classified (category evidence decision 5), its **members** in their order, each the candidate, its judgments as the last judge wrote them and its facts the facts row's at the read, and its standing rejects, newest first, each with the prospect list's name, all as `D14-prospect-lists` defines them, at the clock `Config.Now()` gives once for the read (log 3). It also answers how many of the members have each basis, and the changes since the list's previous open (`D14-prospect-lists`), and records nothing: it is the peek (basis decisions 4, 8 and 11). `OpenProspectList` answers exactly what `ReadProspectList` would at its one clock reading and, in the same write, records the open: the time, the caller, the definition, the answered members with their bases and the passing set, so two opens of one list take turns and each compares against the one before (basis decisions 7 and 8). It refuses an empty user id `ErrUnreachable`, as no consumer sends one, and a list it does not hold, a deleted one included, `ErrNotFound`, recording nothing. Membership is one query over the judgments joined to the candidates and, by a left join, to the facts rows, every fact filter and order key reading the facts row and a missing row reading every fact unknown, cut by the size, beside one count (cohorts decision 25); the store holds no more than the members.

`ListProspectLists` and `ListProspectListsReversed` are the prospect list windows the `prospect_lists` tool and the landing read, in **prospect list order**, name then id, each in byte order (log 18), a window after a position or before it, as the passes' are.

`Reject` records a reject or an unreject. A reject of a pair that has no standing reject, or an unreject of one that has, records one record and answers it; a reject of a pair already rejected writes nothing and answers the record that stands; an unreject of a pair with no standing reject writes nothing and answers a record of kind `unreject` naming the prospect list, the candidate and the prospect list's name, with no why, user or time (log 27), so every reject and unreject is safe to repeat (cohorts decisions 10 and 18). It refuses a blank why `ErrInvalid`, a prospect list the store does not hold, a deleted one included, `ErrNotFound`, then a candidate it does not hold `ErrNotFound`, then a merged candidate `ErrInvalid` (log 6). `Rejects` answers every record naming a candidate, active or merged, newest first (cohorts decision 11). A prospect list's standing rejects are listed whatever the candidate's status (`D14-prospect-lists`), so a reject of a candidate that is later merged keeps standing on its prospect list's read and cannot be cleared, since `Reject` refuses a merged candidate `ErrInvalid` either way; a merged candidate is no member of any prospect list, so the row only shows.

## Parks

`ParkRules` answers the stored rules and the counties known (`D18-parks`): the counties the label rows name together with those the newest applied derivation read, so a county given labels before scrapes holds it is still listed. `SetParkRules` edits them in one write that also records the parks pass of origin `rules` the edit starts: any of the three numbers, the walk ceiling, and one county's label rows replaced whole, an empty list leaving that county to the fallbacks; a value a `parks` validator refuses is `ErrInvalid` and a running parks pass `ErrBusy`, either changing nothing, so an edit lands exactly when its re-derivation is recorded.

A parks pass reads its inputs a piece at a time and writes once. `ActiveAddressKeys` answers one page of the active candidates' address keys, in id order after a cursor, keys only; `PriorParks` every park held, active or gone, with its parcels' PINs, which the matching needs whole. `ApplyParks` then writes the derivation in one transaction: each update replaces a park's center, radius, ZIP, building value, count and parcels, keeping its id, county, town, number and mint time; each new park is minted; each gone park is marked, keeping its parcels; the derivation's counties and run are recorded beside the rules; and the pass ends done with its counts and its parcel run. Nothing else writes a park, so a parks pass that fails leaves the parks as the last done one left them, and a derivation is never half applied.

`ListParks` and `ListParksReversed` are the park windows the `parks` tool and the parks page read, in the park order under one of three sorts (`D18-parks`), after or before a position, the sort key and id of the edge of the window before; under minutes the sort key of an unknown drive time is empty, and it sorts last. `GetPark` and `ParkParcels` answer one park and its parcels; a park's name and drive minutes are worked out at the read. `CountParks`, `ParkCountiesHeld`, `ParkCountiesRead` and `ParksRun` answer the parks page's summary and county choices.

## Walks

The walk's records, a place found (`store.Place`), a walk of a park (`store.ParkWalk`) and a candidate found in a park (`store.FoundCandidate`), and the methods `RecordSweep`, `FinishWalk`, `FailWalk`, `PassWalks`, `ParkWalks`, `ParkPlaces`, `ParkCandidates`, `CandidatePark` and `WalkCeiling` are declared here; their behavior, and the places attach rule `RecordSweep` applies, is `D19-walk`'s, as `Attach`'s is `D12-ingest`'s.

## Enrichments

`AddEnrichments` records a pass's fetched results in one write, in the order given, each stamped with the clock, and rewrites each written candidate's facts row in the same write, and the display of each candidate it records a `drive_route` result on, by `D12-ingest`'s rule at the store's origin, so a route that placed the business to the building shows as its address at once (R-HTD3-0YUG); it skips a result naming a candidate that is merged or unknown, so no result is ever on a merged candidate, as no link is, and it answers what it recorded; were every candidate sharing an inputs merged between the pass's selection and its write, that result is skipped and recorded nowhere, and the inputs may be fetched again for the survivor, in the same pass when its id lies after the cursor, or in a later one. It accepts what the registry's `Valid` accepts for the kind (`D16-enrichers`) with non-empty inputs and a finite, non-negative cost, and keeps `Found` compacted, as given otherwise; anything else, an unknown kind, a found that is not JSON or not the kind's, empty inputs, or a cost that is negative, NaN or infinite (which SQLite would store as NULL and `cost`'s not-null refuses), is a value no consumer sends, refused `ErrUnreachable` with nothing recorded. The store does no UTF-8 repair of its own: the enrichers make `Found` with `json.Marshal`, which already replaces each invalid byte with U+FFFD. That a merge racing it leaves the result moved or unrecorded, never on the merged candidate, is `D12-ingest`'s, which states the store's writes behave as one after another. `Enrichments` answers every result on a candidate, merged or not, newest first, each as it was stored, whichever write recorded it.

`Derive` runs one derived kind over one window of the active candidates in id order after a cursor, in one write: for each it builds the candidate's subject from the records (`D16-enrichers`), and records the kind's result, at the clock, when the inputs are not empty and either the candidate is due or the call is forced, rewriting the facts row beside it. It answers the last id it examined, empty once the walk is over, and how many results it recorded. An enrich derives what is due; a full ingest, and a merge or a split for the candidates it touches, force it, so every derived kind is recomputed whatever its inputs (`D06-passes`, `D12-ingest`). The derived kinds run inside the store (decided for this delivery; enrichers decision 9, which has a merge or a split re-run them in the same call, and decision 15 with its decisions log): a merge or a split re-runs them in the same write as its moves and its re-judge, as the store already judges, and the enrichers package is pure functions the store calls.

`DueEnrichments` examines one page of the active candidates, in id order after a cursor, and answers those of them due for a kind, each with its inputs now, so a fetching pass groups them by inputs without reading them again, and the page's last id as the next cursor, so a pass over a space with few due candidates still reads one page per call and never walks the whole space in one read. The store answers whatever inputs the kind names: a display email with no domain key (Gmail and the like) has empty inputs for `website_check` and is never due (website log P12, enrichers decision 3). `EnrichmentMark` answers an opaque mark of what is recorded so far, and `EnrichmentAfter` the last result of a kind with given inputs recorded after a mark, which is how a pass, reading the mark when its work begins, tells inputs it already fetched across batches without holding a set of them (`D15-website-check`).

`DueAmong` and `DeriveAmong` are the targeted forms (site text decision 13): each takes the ids an `enrich_target` walks, a prospect list's members in their order or one candidate, and a force flag. `DueAmong` answers, in the order given and each once, the ids naming an active candidate whose inputs for the kind are not empty and that is due, or every such id when forced, each with its inputs, so a fetched or prompts kind gets its targets without a window; `DeriveAmong` records the derived kind's result for the same set in one write, as a `Derive` window over those ids alone would, and answers how many it recorded. Due and force mean the same for every kind, the same inputs rule a corpus enrich uses, so a forced derived kind records a fresh result for each target as a full ingest does. An id the store does not hold, or a merged candidate's, is skipped rather than refused, since the targets are fixed when the pass is recorded and a ruling may merge one while it runs. `Subject` answers the subject `D16-enrichers` defines for one candidate, its kept pages and its facts row's facts among it (brief decisions 3 and 4), and its display street and phone, its facts row's website source and its newest website check whose inputs share a URL with its current inputs, its Places URL and its email's domain (website search decision 3, R-CZDT-QYO8), and the newest `site_text` result against its facts row's website and the inputs of its newest `website_search` result (empty sites decision 2, R-EDDP-FK2P), which a prompts kind makes a run's input and its inputs from; for `site_text` the inputs are the website URL its facts row holds, so a website check that lands elsewhere, or a search whose site is confirmed, makes the candidate due again, and for `brief` a fingerprint of its pages' URLs and their newest copies' numbers, so a newer copy that `add_page` or a `site_text` run keeps makes it due again and nothing else does (brief decisions 1 and 2).

`GetFacts` answers a candidate's facts row, with its records read beside it, `ErrNotFound` when it has none: no result yet, merged, or not yet filled after `0012`. `FillFacts` writes the missing rows of one page of the active candidates per call, the engine's `StartFill` (`D06-passes`) walking every page once each start, in the background after the start sends `READY=1` (`D03-serve`), every pass refused `busy` while it runs and a reader seeing each candidate's row as the previous start left it until the fill reaches its page, from the newest results each candidate already holds, and touches no row already written but those of candidates holding a `drive_route` result, which it re-projects at the store's origin, so a changed origin takes effect at the start that changes it, and those whose minutes and source disagree, minutes with no source or a source with no minutes, which only the previous release writes; and it re-projects every row's headcounts and brief labels, so a row written before `0020` gets them at the first start after it, and a row the previous release wrote or rewrote after a rollback gets them back at the start of the roll forward (revenue bands decision 19, R-EKP3-Q6IV); and it rewrites the display of every candidate of its page holding a `drive_route` result at the store's origin, so a routed address a changed origin no longer stands behind leaves the display at the start that changes it, and returns at a start that restores the origin (R-HUKZ-EQL5); it is safe at every start; a failing store answers it `ErrUnreachable` and it changes nothing, as every store call does then (R-OD8H-2EDG). Every member a prospect list read answers carries the facts row's facts, read in the same read, or every fact unknown when there is no row, beside its judgments.

A merge moves the merged candidate's results to the survivor with its links, keeping the order they were recorded in, deletes the merged candidate's facts row, and then records on the survivor a fresh result of every derived kind whose inputs are not empty; a split leaves the results on the candidate it split and records fresh results, likewise, for both; every other write but `AddEnrichments`, `Derive` and `DeriveAmong` leaves the results alone, and only those, `FillFacts` and a merge or a split write a facts row (website decision 21, enrichers decision 15, `D12-ingest`).

## Pages

`AddPage` records one copy of a page on an active candidate (site text decisions 1 to 3): the URL, title, text, reason and size the caller fetched, who added it, the clock as when it was fetched, and a number the database mints. A copy is readable, its reason empty, or unreadable (page text outcome 3), its text then empty and its reason one of the website check's three codes, `website.ReasonScripted`, `website.ReasonRedirect` and `website.ReasonEmpty` (`D15-website-check`); the store names `internal/website`'s constants rather than declaring its own, since it already imports that package and the codes are the reader's to define. An unreadable copy is a page in every other respect: its URL counts against `PageCap` as a distinct URL, `CountPages` counts it, `Pages` and `GetPage` answer it with its reason, and a merge moves it with its reason (`D12-ingest`); what reads it as unreadable is the brief's inputs and the tools (`D16-enrichers`, `D10-tools`). It is refused `ErrNotFound` for a candidate the store does not hold, `ErrInvalid` for a merged one, and `ErrPageCap` when the URL is new to the candidate and the candidate already holds `PageCap` (20) distinct URLs; a URL it already holds is always accepted, since a newer copy adds no URL. The cap is counted inside the write, so two adds racing cannot carry a candidate past it. An empty URL or user id, a negative size, a reason that is not one of the three codes, or a reason beside text is a value no consumer sends, refused `ErrUnreachable` like the others. `AddPage` writes nothing else, no enrichment result, no facts row and no pass, since a page is tied to no pass and no run (site text decision 4). The tool that calls it checks the host, the cap and the fetch first and answers each with its own sentence (`D10-tools`, `D15-website-check`).

`Pages` answers a candidate's newest copy of each URL, newest first by number, without the text, which the `candidate` tool and the candidate page list; `GetPage` one copy with its text, by the candidate and its number, `ErrNotFound` when that number is not on that candidate, so a number a merge moved is reached only through the survivor; and `CountPages` the distinct URLs and the copies a candidate holds, which a `site_text` run's result and its pass's `Pages` count read when the run ends (`D06-passes`; settled for this delivery, S6). A merge moves the other candidate's pages to the survivor, keeping each number, and a split leaves them on the candidate it split (`D12-ingest`); no other write touches a page. The subject's pages are a second read of the same rows, with their text: the newest copy of each URL, in the order the URLs were first kept (`D16-enrichers`, brief decision 3), which `Pages`, newest first and without the text, does not answer. It is no method of its own, only part of the subject, and the store reads it only for a `Prompter` kind (P1, recorded below).

The refetch work list is the repair's (page text outcome 6, decision D4, `D06-passes`): `0027_page_reasons.sql` fills it, in the same migration that adds `pages.reason`, with the number of every page that was the newest copy of its candidate and URL and whose size was exactly 262144, the old read limit, so the list is fixed when the migration runs and no page stored afterwards is ever on it. `PendingRefetches` answers its pending entries after a number, up to `n`, in number order, each the page as `GetPage` answers it now, under the candidate a merge moved it to, with its text; `SettleRefetch` settles one with its outcome, `RefetchRead`, `RefetchUnreadable` or `RefetchFailed`, and a settled entry is never pending again, which is the repair's idempotence: a second run over a settled store finds nothing to fetch. Settling an entry already settled answers nil and keeps the first outcome; a number not on the list is `ErrNotFound`, and an outcome that is not one of the three is `ErrUnreachable`, a value no consumer sends. The repair adds its copies through `AddPage` alone, so `SettleRefetch` touches the list and never a page (R-WNFA-Q8TO). No read answers an entry's outcome: the counts the repair reports are its own (`D06-passes`), and the stored outcomes are kept for the operator to query.

## Campaign uses

`AddCampaignUse`, `RejectCampaignUse` and `DeliverCampaignUse` record what a delivered campaigns event told (`D06-passes`): the candidate, the campaign, the prospect list or the reason the event carried, and the event's own time, which the store keeps in UTC to the second. Each first follows `merged_into` from the candidate it is given to the **active end** of its chain, the active candidate it was merged into last, and records there, since campaigns keeps the id it was given and the history must land on the business as it stands now (campaigns log D1). Each creates the use when the candidate holds none of that campaign, so a reject or a delivery told before its add still leaves a use; and each changes only its own part of the use: an add keeps the earliest add, with the prospect list that add named; a reject keeps the first rejection, with its reason; a delivery keeps the latest delivery. So a repeated event changes nothing, and events told in any order leave the same use (campaigns decision 16). An empty candidate or campaign is refused `ErrInvalid` and a candidate the store does not hold `ErrNotFound`, each recording nothing; a zero time is a value no consumer sends, since every bus event carries its time.

`CampaignUses` answers the uses recorded under exactly the id it is given, following no pointer: a merge this release records moves the merged candidate's uses to the survivor (`D12-ingest`), so it answers none after that merge (a merge the previous release recorded leaves them behind, Known limits), and the candidate tool and page show the survivor's. They come newest add first, a use with no add known after every other, ties by campaign id, and the read is one candidate's rows on the table's key. No other write touches a use but a merge, which combines the merged candidate's uses into the survivor's (`D12-ingest`); a split, an exclude and an include leave them where they are, and nothing removes one.

## Reads

`GetCandidate` answers any candidate, active or merged: a merged candidate's record carries its status and the id it was merged into. `Links`, `Estimates`, `GetJudgments`, `Rulings` and `Rejects` answer what the store holds for one candidate; each tells an unknown candidate (`ErrNotFound`) from a known one with nothing to show, as `Pages` and `CampaignUses` do, except `GetJudgments`, which answers `ErrNotFound` for a candidate not yet judged too, and `GetFacts`, which answers `ErrNotFound` for a candidate with no facts row. A candidate's judgments are the last judge's, and its facts are `GetFacts`' at the read. Links come not gone first, then newest filing first, so the freshest evidence leads; rulings and reject records newest first. `ListCandidates` is one window of the active candidates newest first, those after a position, the created text and id of the last candidate of the window before, at most `n`, optionally only those one of whose names contains a text, case-insensitively, those at a ZIP, or those one of whose links has a phone key (decision 14).
