Beside `ListPasses` and `ListProspectLists` sit their reversed windows, which the landing's backward step and its last window read (`D08-pages`): `ListPassesReversed` answers at most `n` of the passes that precede a position in pass order, nearest first, and `ListProspectListsReversed` likewise in prospect list order; an empty id there means the end of the order, so the reversed window from no position is the oldest passes, or the prospect lists last in order, last first. The position need not be any entry's. The tools never call them. Each window is ordered and cut by the database on an index (appkit's D25, the suite's Storage convention): a read holds at most the window it was asked for, and the tools and pages ask for one more than they show to learn whether more follow. The store knows nothing of tokens; the tools and pages encode and decode them with `appkit/paging` from the position fields an entry carries (`D10-tools`, `D08-pages`). All lists are never nil.

## Errors

`ErrNotFound`, `ErrBusy`, `ErrDelivered`, `ErrEnded`, `ErrInvalid` and `ErrPageCap` are answers about the records; `ErrInvalid` is the answer of the writes whose arguments come from a person: `Decide`'s, a ruling that is malformed or one the records forbid (`D12-ingest`), `CreateProspectList`'s and `EditProspectList`'s, a part a validator refuses, `Reject`'s, a blank why or a merged candidate, `AddPage`'s, a merged candidate, and the campaign use writes', an empty candidate or campaign, which the delivery skips before it calls (`D06-passes`). `ErrPageCap` is `AddPage`'s alone, a URL past the cap, which the tool answers with a sentence naming the cap (`D10-tools`). Every other error satisfies `errors.Is(err, ErrUnreachable)`, and every consumer answers it with `Unreachable` (a tool's `unavailable`, a page's unavailable answer, a delivery's `Fail`, a pass failed `store`), never quoting the cause. A done context, a failing handle, a random source that fails and a write given a value no consumer sends all end there, the last changing nothing. No error variable is added for prospect lists.

## `db status`

`candidates db status` writes what appkit's `db.Status` writes for `state/candidates.db` and the root package's `Migrations()`, and creates nothing.

## Bounded work

candidates follows `shape/`'s Bounded work (P16 to P18): disk and wall time may grow with the space; memory and the work of one call may not. Every walk over a set that grows with the space is the caller's, a page per call, as `Derive` already was: `JudgeAll`, `MarkGone`, `FillFacts` and `DueEnrichments` each take a cursor and an `n`, read at most `n` keys after the cursor, the active candidates in id order or, for `MarkGone`, its scraper's links in source id order, in one transaction, and answer the last key read as the next cursor, empty once the walk is over (R-KUDX-LYIQ); `PrunePasses` removes at most `n` passes per call and answers how many; `ActiveAddressKeys` reads at most `n` active candidates after its cursor and answers their address keys and the last id read, so a parks pass holds the candidate keys as keys only, read a batch at a time, and the parcel rows it streams are its own to bound (`D06-passes`). Every caller passes `passes.BatchSize`. `JudgeAll` also takes the judge time, which the pass reads once, so one judge's pages carry one time. `MarkGone` no longer answers the candidates it touched: they are those holding a link it marked, which `D12-ingest` names. A read bounded by one key, a candidate's own links, rulings, enrichments or pages, is one query, folded as read. No statement, transaction or open cursor spans the space, and no call holds more than its page; what each unit holds is recorded below (The units and their bounds), with the one departure, a prospect list's passing set.

## Known limits

- **A rollback across `0015` shows search results unmarked** (website search decision 14): to the previous release a `website_search` result is a row of a kind its registry lacks, which its enrich never walks and its candidate page and `candidate` tool show as the found object's members as stored, never marked failed.
- **A rollback across `0015` drops a searched site from the row it re-projects**: the previous release's own re-projection of a candidate takes `website` and `website_url` from the newest check alone, so a site the search found drops out of that row, and out of the `website` filter, until this release next writes it; until then its judge reads a found site in that row as the check's, live evidence this release's judge would not count (`D13-judging`).
- **A roll forward across a rollback may leave a stale source**: a row the previous release rewrote may carry a `website_source` that no longer agrees with its site, `search` beside the check's `none`, or empty beside a site it wrote, until the candidate's next result or an `ingest`, whose forced `link_facts` walk re-projects every row (`D06-passes`); so an `ingest` follows a roll forward across a rollback.
- **A rollback across `0016` loses campaign history meanwhile**: the previous release accepts no campaigns event, so what campaigns tells while it runs is recorded nowhere, and a business added to a campaign then shows no use and passes the campaign filter after the roll forward until a later event of that campaign names it.
- **A merge on the previous release leaves the uses behind**: it does not know `campaign_uses`, so a merge it records leaves the merged candidate's uses under the merged id, where `CampaignUses` of the survivor does not see them and the campaign filter does not count them; a later write follows the pointer to the survivor as usual.
- **A prospect list's campaign filter is unknown to the previous release**: it decodes a list's stored filters into its own filters type, which ignores `min_campaign_age_days`, so it reads such a prospect list as though the filter were absent, and an edit it makes of that prospect list's filters writes them back without it.
- **`force` cannot clear a wrong found site** (website search decision 7): a search that finds nothing never unseats a confirmed one, its `none` deciding only where no search confirmed a site (website states outcome 3); only a later confirmed search that is not inert replaces it, or a check that finds a site on a host other than the one a takeover search ran against, unless that search ran against a site a search had decided.
- **A row keeps the previous release's website reading until it is re-projected** (website states, decided by the delivery): no migration rewrites a facts row, so a row the previous release wrote keeps its reading, a check's `none` among them where this release reads `unknown`, and a search that found nothing unread as `none`, until something rewrites the row: the candidate's next result, a full `ingest`, whose forced `link_facts` walk re-projects every active candidate's row (R-EAXW-O0LB, `D06-passes`), or the start's `FillFacts`, which re-projects a whole row only for a candidate holding a `drive_route` result or whose minutes and minutes source disagree (R-EKP3-Q6IV); so a full `ingest` follows the deploy. After a rollback the previous release reads such rows unchanged and re-projects any row it rewrites by its own rule, under which a check's `none` decides `none` and a search that found nothing decides nothing, until this release next writes the row. Among such rows are those release-72 wrote for a search whose named site redirected back onto the site on record, its source turned to `search` at a URL on the host already on record, the same URL or one differing only in its path or query; the same rewrites, a full `ingest`'s forced walk, the candidate's next result, or `FillFacts` where it re-projects, give them back to the result that decided before, as the search ran against the site its confirmation landed on and so is inert (empty sites decision 17).
- **A facts row's drive is stale between an address change and the next result**: an `Attach` that changes a candidate's display street, city, state or ZIP changes no facts row (R-D49F-A1N0), so a route found from the old address keeps deciding the row's minutes until the next write of the row, which the ingest's follow-on enrich makes when its derived kinds record a result on the candidate, the changed links giving `link_facts` other inputs (`D06-passes`); a read in between shows the old route.
- **A rollback across `0018` can leave a stale route label** (drive route outcome 15, decided by the delivery): the previous release does not know `drive_route`; its facts upsert writes `minutes` from its newest `drive_minutes` result and `drive_route_at` as for any kind among the candidate's results, and leaves `minutes_source`, `miles` and `route_address` as this release wrote them (R-38GK-FZPH). So a row it rewrites while it runs may read ZIP minutes beside the source `route` and the old route's miles and address, its drive time filter reading the ZIP minutes; a row it inserts or fills with minutes carries no source; and a row whose minutes it clears, its ZIP gone from the table, keeps the source `zip`. Each stays so after the roll forward until the candidate's next result is recorded or the start's `FillFacts`, which re-projects every candidate holding a `drive_route` result and every row whose minutes and source disagree (R-EKP3-Q6IV); those cover every row the previous release can leave wrong, so every row is right again once the roll forward is up. A `drive_route` result is, to the previous release, a row of a kind its registry lacks, which its enrich never walks and its candidate page and `candidate` tool show as the found object's members as stored, never marked failed.

- **A rollback across `0017` hides what this release wrote** (funnel rename decision 17, D6 of its delivery log): the previous release reads `cohorts`, `rejects.cohort`, `campaign_uses.cohort` and `assessments`, all intact as of the upgrade. Invisible to it are the prospect lists created or edited since; the rejects made since, which it lists for their candidate with an empty list and no name and which stand in no list's reject set; the campaign uses recorded since, which it reads with an empty list, since this release writes the list into `campaign_uses.prospect_list` and leaves `cohort` empty (R-B9KK-F52P); and the judgments written since, `assessments` staying as of the upgrade until its own daily judge rewrites it. A prospect list deleted since reappears to it. While it runs, it records the adds the new campaigns tells with an empty list, since that `campaign.member.added` carries `prospect_list`, an attribute it does not read. It answers the passes recorded since with this release's kind, origin and trigger words and its own judged count as 0. Nothing is lost: every row stays in this release's tables.

- **A roll forward after a rollback does not copy again**: `0017` has run and does not run twice, so what the previous release wrote meanwhile is invisible or stale here: the lists it created and the edits it made to migrated lists, written into `cohorts` and unseen here, so a migrated list it deleted reappears here, the rejects it wrote into `rejects.cohort`, the uses it recorded with an empty `prospect_list`, the passes it recorded, answered with its kind, origin and trigger words and `judged` 0 until the prune removes them, and the judgments, stale until the next judge. The recipe is to run `judge`, or a full `ingest`, once the roll forward is up.

- **A rollback across `0019` ignores the basis filter** (basis decision 3): the previous release decodes a list's stored filters into its own filters type, which has no `basis`, so it reads a list filtered by basis as though the filter were absent and shows members of every basis.
- **An edit under the rollback drops the basis filter for good**: an edit the previous release makes of a list's filters re-encodes them from its own type, which drops `basis`, so the list keeps no basis filter after the roll forward until the owner sets it again (R-3KRM-IU9T).
- **The previous release records no open**: its page and tool open a list without writing `opens`, so the first open after the roll forward compares against the last open recorded before the rollback, and its changes span the whole rollback.
- **Opens are unbounded** (basis decision 10): one row per open, each holding the answered members and the passing set, the whole matching set before the size cut and so the bulk of a row, never pruned until a retention policy is set, so a list opened often grows its history by one row each time.
- **A rollback across `0020` shows stale headcounts after the roll forward** (revenue bands decision 19): the previous release names none of `0020`'s columns. Its facts upsert names every `facts` column the first nineteen files make, in its insert and its update, so a row it inserts holds `headcounts` and `brief_labels` at `[]` and a row it updates keeps those this release wrote, however its newer `link_facts` or `brief` result differs (R-LV4L-IK5X); each stays so after the roll forward until the candidate's next result is recorded or the start's `FillFacts`, which re-projects every row's headcounts and brief labels (R-EKP3-Q6IV), so they are right again once the roll forward is up.
- **A rollback across `0022` ignores the trend filter** (headcount trend decision 13): the previous release decodes a list's stored filters into its own filters type, which has no `trend` or `trend_unknown`, so it reads a list filtered by trend as though the filter were absent and shows members of every trend.
- **An edit under the rollback drops the trend filter for good**: an edit the previous release makes of a list re-encodes its filters from its own type, which drops `trend` and `trend_unknown`, so the list keeps no trend filter after the roll forward until the owner sets it again (R-PC0D-41S5).
- **A rollback across `0022` leaves a list ordered by trend unreadable until its order is changed**: the previous release keeps a stored order term it does not know, since it decodes the order with `json.Unmarshal` and normalizes only an empty order, and builds the members query from a table of the order fields it knows, in which `trend` has no entry, so the query it builds for such a list is not valid SQL and its read and open of the list fail with an error matching `ErrUnreachable`, so neither the page nor the tools show its members, while the landing still lists it. An edit it makes of the list's order replaces the term and the list reads again; an edit of anything else keeps it. Checked against release-66's store, the previous release: its ordering puts an empty expression before `IS NULL ASC` for a field it lacks, which the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 refuses as a syntax error.
- **The previous release's judge leaves a stale series and trend**: it upserts `judgments` naming only the columns the first twenty-one files give it, in its insert and its update, so a candidate it judges for the first time holds no series and an unknown trend, and one this release judged keeps the series and trend this release wrote beside the headcount and revenue estimate the previous rules give (R-E8FQ-9PRL); so after it judges, a card may show a series and trend that disagree with its new headcount. After the roll forward they read as written, beside an older rules version, until the next judge, which the daily trigger runs; a judge over everything repairs them at once (headcount trend decision 14).
- **A rollback across `0021` can leave stale key rows after the roll forward** (form5500 decision 23): the previous release rewrites a link's reading and `name_key` and leaves its `ein_key` and side rows as this release last wrote them, so a link it rewrote has a reading with no `EINKey` or `NameKeys` beside an `ein_key` and side rows that still name the keys it once held. They are harmless: the indexes only find candidate links, and a hit counts only when the link's decoded reading holds the key, so a stale `ein_key` or side row never matches; the link holds only its reading's `NameKey`, as one the previous release inserted or one written before `0021` does, until the next full ingest under the new rules version rewrites its reading, its `ein_key` and its side rows.
- **The previous release's judge drops the `headcount` estimate and leaves the headcount behind**: its judge deletes every estimate of each candidate it judges and inserts only its sources' estimates, under its own rules, so the `headcount` estimate is gone from every candidate it judged, and upserts `judgments` naming only the columns `0017` gave it, in its insert and its update, so a candidate it judges for the first time holds `headcount_low` and `headcount_high` NULL and `headcount_basis` empty, and one this release judged keeps the headcount this release wrote beside the revenue estimate the previous rules give (R-LV4L-IK5X). After the roll forward those judgments read as written, a stale headcount beside an older rules version, until the next judge; so a full ingest follows a roll forward across a rollback, as `0020`'s own deploy needs one (revenue bands decision 20).
- **A rollback across this release's full ingest leaves almost every candidate unranged** (revenue bands decisions 12, 13 and 20): a judge reads the readings stored on the links and never re-reads a row (`D13-judging`), and after this release's full ingest every link holds a reading under this release's rules, in which PPP gives no estimate and a `private` or unknown-class carrier gives no truck estimate. The previous release's judge, a judge pass, a ruling's re-judge or the daily cron fire, builds its estimates from those readings and forms no `headcount` estimate, so every candidate but a for-hire or mixed carrier is judged with no estimate and a nil revenue low, and drops out of every list that orders or filters on revenue. It stays so until a full ingest runs under the previous release, which re-reads the rows into its own readings; and after rolling forward again, the links hold the previous release's readings, carrying no headcount, so another full ingest under this release is needed before the headcounts and the `headcount` estimates return.
- **A rollback across the OSHA ITA change judges a business of several sites by its largest** (OSHA ITA outcomes 12 and 18): the previous release, release-63, reads the OSHA ITA links' stored readings as it reads every link's, and its judge chooses among the newest headcounts of one kind the greatest count, not the sum of the sites (`D13-judging`), so Design Ready Controls is judged by its largest site's count rather than the 523 its sites sum to, and its judgment and `headcount` estimate stay so after the roll forward until the next judge. A summary entered twice still counts once under it, since the greater of two equal counts is one of them.
- **A rollback across the OSHA ITA change ingests no OSHA ITA run**: the previous release's registry holds no `osha_ita` source, so it skips a delivered `scrape.finished` of that scraper and its full ingest reads no `osha_ita` run; the OSHA ITA links stand as this release last wrote them, neither updated nor marked gone, and a run that finished meanwhile is read only by a full ingest after the roll forward or by the next run's own ingest.
- **A rollback across the OSHA ITA change drops the hours from the facts rows until a fresh `link_facts` result**: the previous release decodes stored readings, `link_facts` results and facts rows with `json.Unmarshal` into its own types, which ignore the members they lack, and what it writes lacks them. Its start's `FillFacts` leaves a row whose headcounts it reads as unchanged as it is, `hours` included, since it compares the decoded headcounts, unless it repairs the row for another column, a row it finds missing or a candidate holding a `drive_route` result whose row differs elsewhere, which it rewrites whole without `hours`; but any write of a row it makes, a result recorded on the candidate, writes the row's headcounts with no `hours`, and its enrich walk, which reads every candidate's `link_facts` inputs as changed (`D16-enrichers`), records on every candidate a `link_facts` result whose headcounts carry no `hours`, so the walk strips the hours from every row it reaches. A reading it writes is never of an OSHA ITA row, so it lacks only the four OSHA ITA fields that row alone sets, and loses nothing. After the roll forward, the start's `FillFacts` restores the hours of every row whose newest `link_facts` result this release recorded (R-EKP3-Q6IV, R-H6LD-6IMC); a candidate on which the previous release recorded a newer one reads its headcounts with nil `Hours` until this release records its next, which its next enrich walk does, the inputs again reading as changed, and a full ingest's forced `link_facts` walk does for every candidate (`D06-passes`).
- **A read during a judge may mix two judges** (P15 (a), after the `us` full ingest failed `store` three times): a judge writes page by page, one `JudgeAll` call each, so a page or a tool read while it runs may show some candidates judged anew and others as the judge before left them, and a judge that fails keeps the pages it wrote, the rest as the judge before left them, until the next judge. A judge was one transaction over the whole space before, and held the writer 48 to 67 seconds on `us`, where litestream's checkpoint takes the write lock against appkit's five-second busy timeout.
- **A unit busy for 31 seconds of pauses fails its pass**: past `WriteRetries` retries the call answers `ErrUnreachable` and the pass fails `store`, as every busy write did before; a lock held longer than the pauses and the busy timeouts between them is not waited out.
- **A rollback across `0023` keeps the parks and forgets them**: the previous release names none of `0023`'s tables, so it neither reads nor writes a park, and no tool or page of it offers one; it records, ends and prunes passes naming only its own columns, the new ones taking their defaults (R-6IBL-YRUO), so a pass it records reads, after the roll forward, with no parcel run and the eight parks counts 0; it shows a parks pass it finds by its kind word, and its prune removes one like any other pass. A `scrape.finished` of `mn_parcels` delivered meanwhile it skips, as it skips every scraper its registry does not hold, so the parks stay as the last derivation left them until the roll forward's start reads the current run and, finding it differs from the stored derived run, which no prune removes, derives them again.
- After a rollback to release-70, a walk pass reads with its kind word `walk` as stored, and a failed one with the reason `places`, outside release-70's four reasons; the places and park walks tables and the five walk counts are unread; a walked park shows walked by the `0023` columns `RecordSweep` keeps (`D19-walk`); and places links stay, but release-70's display and link facts rules read every link alike, so the next `Attach` or derive touching a matched candidate may let a places link lead its display or add its category, until the walk's release returns and a full ingest or a later write re-derives it.
- **A rollback to release-71 gives a taken-over site back to the check** (empty sites decision 3; empty sites outcome 13): release-71's website decision lets a newest check that found a site decide whatever the searches found, so a row it re-projects for a company whose takeover search decided here reads the check's site, with the source `check`, in its facts, its filters and its judge's evidence, until this release next writes the row: the company's next result or a full `ingest`. A row it does not rewrite keeps this release's reading, and this release's projection, a function of the results alone, gives the takeover back when it next writes the row. A rollback to release-72 is safe (empty sites decision 17): release-72 reads the same `website` and `confirmation` members by its own rule, which judges a takeover by the recorded case, so a row it re-projects for a company whose search named a site that redirected back onto the site on record reads that search's URL with the source `search` again, until this release next writes the row, and, the reverse, a row taken over here by a search whose recorded case is `stands` but whose confirmation landed on another host reads the check's site with the source `check` again, until this release next writes the row; nothing in the schema or a payload differs between the two.
- **A rollback to release-71 makes some searched companies due again** (decision 4 finding of the empty sites delivery; empty sites outcome 13): release-71 computes a search's inputs by its own rule, the name, city and state fingerprint for a site the search found, which differs from the inputs this release recorded for a company searched under the empty-site condition, or keeping them since (`D16-enrichers`); so such a company, and a company whose takeover search decided, whose facts row reads the source `search`, reads due again for `website_search` under release-71 while its row reads that source, and is searched again at the owner's next ask over it, a paid re-run and no error. A company whose site the check found and site text did not show empty reads not due there, as before.
- **A rollback to release-74 leaves the new source columns stale until the next full ingest** (facts from the candidate): release-74 names none of `0026`'s columns and writes each candidate's display and `people` by its own rule, so a candidate it rewrites keeps the sources, record keys and other values this release last wrote beside a display they may no longer describe, its people without `Source` or `Key`, and a facts row it rewrites keeps its known since and newest filing sources and checked URL from before; nothing reads wrongly to release-74, and after the roll forward each row reads stale until this release next writes it, which the next full `ingest` does for every active candidate (R-FLA3-7ARP, `D12-ingest`).
- **Release-71's passes count no search case**: it names none of `0025`'s columns, so every pass it records, a `website_search` pass included, reads `SearchDifferent`, `SearchStands` and `SearchNone` 0 here (R-WM60-C4VT, R-WOLT-3OD7), and the results it recorded carry no case for the candidate page to show.
- **A takeover the byte match missed waits for its row's next write** (empty sites decision 19): no migration rewrites a facts row, so a row that fell back to the check's site because the search's recorded website and the check's URL differed in bytes, a later `again` run's confirmed host lost or a URL differing by a query string, keeps that reading until this release next writes the row: the company's next result, a full `ingest` or `FillFacts`. The rebuild from the stored members also heals the rows written during the release-72 window whose URL differs from the check's by a query string. A rollback to release-73 reads the byte match again, so a row it re-projects for such a company reads the check's site with the source `check` until this release next writes the row.
- **A rollback across `0027` reads unreadable pages as empty ones** (page text decision D3): the previous release names neither `pages.reason` nor `page_refetches`, and reads a page by the columns the first twenty-six files give it, so an unreadable copy reads to it as a page with an empty text, which enters its brief's inputs as one, so a brief it runs meanwhile reads that empty page. Its inserts leave `reason` NULL, which reads here as readable, and its merge moves a page by its candidate alone, keeping the reason. It neither reads nor settles the work list, so after the roll forward the repair resumes with the entries still pending; a page it stored meanwhile is never on the list.
- **A pending entry may no longer be its URL's newest copy**: the list is fixed at migration, so a newer copy `add_page` stores before the repair reaches the entry, or a merge that brings two candidates' copies of one URL together, leaves the entry naming an older copy; the repair still fetches that URL once more and adds a newer copy, which costs one fetch and changes nothing the brief would not read anyway.

## Recorded decisions

- **A store fails only through its handle's `SetFailing`**, the one way candidates' test discipline provokes a database failure (candidates/AGENTS.md); what a store answers once its handle is closed is not contract, since no test may close the store to make it fail. A busy write is not a failure of the handle: a test provokes it by holding a write transaction open on a second `db.Open` handle of the same path, as the read during a write is proved (R-5BN4-KSWD), and a failing handle is never busy, so it pauses nothing (R-DYC6-74HH).

- The schema is twenty-seven migrations: seven, one per table in decision 16's order, named as the scaffold's `AGENTS.md` names them, an eighth that only expands them, a ninth that recreates `assessments` for the two judgments and gives passes a kind and an origin, a tenth that adds the saved searches and their reject records, an eleventh that adds the enrichment results, a twelfth that gives every result its inputs and adds the facts rows, a thirteenth that adds the pages, the passes' target, enrich kind and four counts, and the facts rows' `site_text_at`, a fourteenth that adds the facts rows' `brief` and `brief_at`, a fifteenth that adds the facts rows' `website_source` and `website_search_at`, a sixteenth that adds the campaign uses, a seventeenth that copies the saved searches, the reject records' list, the campaign uses' list and the judgments into tables and columns named for the funnel's stage names and rewrites the pass words (funnel rename decisions 1, 2 and 17), an eighteenth that adds the facts rows' drive source, miles, route address and `drive_route_at` (drive route outcomes 7 and 15), a nineteenth that adds the opens (basis decision 7), a twentieth that adds the facts rows' `headcounts` and `brief_labels` and the judgments' `headcount_low`, `headcount_high` and `headcount_basis` (revenue bands decision 19), a twenty-first that gives the links their EIN key and their name keys (form5500 decisions 15 and 23), a twenty-second that gives the judgments their headcount series and trend (headcount trend decisions 4, 9 and 14), and a twenty-third that adds the parks, their parcels, the park rules, labels and counties, seeded with the seed rules, and the passes' parcel run and eight parks counts (parks decisions 1 to 4); a twenty-fourth only expands, for the walk (`D19-walk`); and a twenty-fifth only expands, giving the passes the search's three case counts (empty sites decision 5); and a twenty-sixth only expands, giving the candidates the source and record of each displayed fact and the other values, and the facts rows the sources of the known-since date and the newest filing and the checked URL (facts from the candidate); and a twenty-seventh only expands, giving the pages their reason and adding the refetch work list, filled once (page text decisions D3 and D4). `0001_candidates.sql`: `candidates` (`id` primary key, `status`, `merged_into`, `name`, `names` as a JSON array, `names_lower`, `street`, `city`, `state`, `zip`, `county`, `phone`, `email`, `people` as a JSON array, `class`, `cargo` as a JSON array, `created`, `updated`), indexed on `status` and `created`. `0002_links.sql`: `links` (`scraper`, `source_id`, `candidate`, `run`, `digest`, `name_key`, `state`, `zip`, `phone_key`, `domain_key`, `reading` as the reading's JSON, `reason`, `confidence`, `first_seen`, `last_seen`, `gone`), unique on `scraper` and `source_id`, indexed on `name_key`, `state` and `zip`, and on `candidate`. `0003_estimates.sql`: `estimates` (`candidate`, `source`, `low`, `high` nullable, `confidence`, `facts` as JSON, `filed`, `formula`), unique on `candidate` and `source`. `0004_assessments.sql`: `assessments` (`candidate` primary key, `fleet`, `low`, `high` nullable, `confidence`, `filed`, `minutes` nullable, `exclusions` as JSON, `excluded`, `base`, `rules`, `scored`), indexed on `base`, the stored ranking number. `0005_decisions.sql`: `decisions` (`seq` integer primary key, `kind`, `candidate`, `other`, `keys` as a JSON array, `why`, `user_id`, `user_email`, `at`), indexed on `candidate` and on `other`. `0006_passes.sql`: `passes` (`id` primary key, `trigger`, `status`, `reason`, `started`, `finished`, the nine counts, `user_id`, `user_email`, `request_id`, `delivery`), indexed on `started`, unique on `delivery` where it is not empty. `0007_zip_drive.sql`: `zip_drive` (`zip` primary key, `minutes`). `0008_windows.sql` (R-YFY0-FLH1): `assessments` gains `effective`, `score` and `score_key`, nullable, and the index `assessments_score` on `score_key` descending and `candidate`; `candidate_cargo` (`candidate`, `cargo`, the pair its primary key, without rowid), indexed on `cargo` and `candidate`, filled from every candidate's `cargo` array; `links` gains the index `links_phone` on `phone_key`, a column it already has; `candidates` the index `candidates_window` on `status`, `created` and `id`; `passes` the index `passes_window` on `started` and `id`, and `passes_trigger` on `trigger`, for the redelivery check. `0009_judgments.sql` (R-35WR-U8HP): `assessments` recreated with every column it had, `confidence` and `base` now nullable and `low`, `filed` and `exclusions` given defaults, beside the new `operating`, `operating_key` (not null, default -1), `evidence` as JSON, `revenue_low`, `revenue_high`, `revenue_confidence`, `known_since`, `government_match`, `newest_filing` and `categories` as JSON, all nullable but the key; the old rows dropped with the old table; `assessments_base` and `assessments_score` recreated on the new table, and `assessments_operating` added on `operating_key` descending and `candidate`; `passes` gains `kind` and `origin`, defaulting to `ingest` and `run`, and every pass whose trigger is `rebuild` is given that word as its kind and origin, which `0017` renames. `0010_cohorts.sql` (R-TIPT-NOAU): `cohorts` (`id` primary key, `name`, `filters` and `ordering` as the JSON of the store's filters and order in their normal form, `size`, `created_user_id`, `created_user_email`, `created`, `changed_user_id`, `changed_user_email`, `changed`, every column not null), indexed on `name` and `id` for the windows (`cohorts_window`, SQLite's default binary collation, so byte order); `rejects` (`seq` integer primary key, `cohort`, `candidate`, `kind`, `user_id`, `user_email`, `at`, `why`), indexed on `cohort`, `candidate` and `seq`, for a list's standing rejects, and on `candidate` and `seq`, for a candidate's records (cohorts decision 23); `assessments` gains `fleet_verdict` (not null, default `unknown`, log 9), `power_units` and `drivers` (nullable, log 20), and loses `assessments_base` and `assessments_score` and then the columns `confidence`, `base`, `effective`, `score`, `score_key`, `filed` and `exclusions` (cohorts decision 16, log 13); `ordering` is named so because `order` is a keyword. `0011_enrichments.sql` (R-J19N-RJ4G): `enrichments` (`seq` integer primary key, `candidate`, `kind`, `found`, the result's state, URL, title, status code, size and error as JSON text (website log P4), `source`, `at`, `cost`, every column not null), indexed on `candidate`, `kind` and `seq`, for a candidate's results and its newest of a kind, and on `kind`, `source` and `seq`, for the results of one source after a mark; `assessments` gains `website` (not null, default `unknown`) and `website_url` (not null, default empty, website log P10) and loses `fleet`, `low` and `high` (website decision 22); `passes` gains `due`, `fetched`, `https`, `http` and `none`, each not null with default 0, so a pass recorded before reads them 0 (decided 2026-10-15: the counts must persist, which goes beyond decision 22's list). `0012_facts.sql` (R-HAM3-FEB0): `enrichments` gains `inputs` (not null, default empty), set to `source` for every `website_check` row, since a website result's source is the domain it was found from, and the index `enrichments_inputs` on `kind`, `inputs` and `seq`, for the results of one inputs after a mark; `facts` (`candidate` primary key, `known_since`, `government_match`, `newest_filing`, `categories`, `fleet_verdict` (not null, default `unknown`), `power_units`, `drivers`, `website` (not null, default `unknown`), `website_url` (not null, default empty), `minutes`, and one last-written column per kind, `drive_minutes_at`, `link_facts_at` and `website_check_at`, each not null with default empty, enrichers settled 8); `passes` gains `derived`, not null with default 0 (enrichers decision 14). `0013_site_text.sql` (R-OZ33-SANJ): `pages` (`seq` integer primary key, the page's number, `candidate`, `url`, `title`, `text`, `size`, `fetched`, `user_id`, `user_email`, every column not null), indexed on `candidate`, `url` and `seq` (`pages_candidate`), for a candidate's newest copy of each URL, its counts and its cap; `passes` gains `target` and `enrich_kind`, text and nullable as site text decision 20 names them, NULL reading as empty, and `runs`, `runs_failed`, `pages` and `cost`, each not null with default 0, `cost` an integer of nano-dollars; `facts` gains `site_text_at`, not null with default empty, as every kind's `_at` is (site text decision 11). `0014_brief.sql` (R-L94M-GG16): `facts` gains `brief`, the summary text, and `brief_at`, each not null with default empty (brief decision 13). `0015_website_search.sql` (R-XLH0-W09U): `facts` gains `website_source`, `check`, `search` or empty for neither, and `website_search_at`, each not null with default empty, and every row whose `website` is `https` or `http` has its `website_source` set to `check` (website search decision 7, S4). `0016_campaign_uses.sql` (R-T493-LKSK): `campaign_uses` (`candidate`, `campaign`, `cohort`, `added`, `rejected`, `reason`, `delivered`, every column text and not null, all but the first two with default empty), the pair `candidate` and `campaign` its primary key, without rowid, so a candidate holds one use of a campaign and its uses are read on the key's prefix (campaigns decisions 3 and 17); its times are RFC 3339 UTC to the second, empty for none. Each `facts` column encodes its fact as the `assessments` column of the same name does: times as RFC 3339 UTC text, NULL or empty for none; `categories` as the JSON array of `sources.Category`, members `Name` and `Source`, which the prospect list query's `$.Name` reads; the counts and minutes as integers, NULL for none; a `_at` column empty for a kind with no result; `brief`, `website_source`, `minutes_source` and `route_address` empty for none; `miles` a real, NULL for none; `headcounts` as a JSON array of objects, one per `sources.Headcount` in the row's order, whose members are `scraper`, `kind`, `count`, an integer, `date`, the `AsOf` as RFC 3339 UTC text to the second, as the row's other dates are kept, the member absent when the headcount has no date, and `hours`, an integer, the member absent when the headcount has no hours, so a headcount of 26 jobs from PPP as of 2020-04-28 is `{"scraper":"ppp","kind":"jobs","count":26,"date":"2020-04-28T00:00:00Z"}` and one of 23 employees from OSHA ITA as of 2025-12-31 over 47840 hours is `{"scraper":"osha_ita","kind":"employees","count":23,"date":"2025-12-31T00:00:00Z","hours":47840}` (`hours` added without a migration, below); and `brief_labels` as a JSON array of strings in the brief's order; each of the two `[]` for none, never NULL. `judgments`' `headcount_low` and `headcount_high` are integers, NULL for none, and `headcount_basis` text, empty for none, as the revenue columns beside them are. `0021_link_keys.sql` (R-EEWA-0TAP): `links` gains `ein_key`, not null with default empty, the reading's `EINKey`, and the index `links_ein` on it; `link_name_keys` (`scraper`, `source_id`, `position`, `name_key`, every column not null), the first three its primary key, one row per element of a link's reading's `NameKeys` with its place in the list, indexed on `name_key` (`link_name_keys_key`). `0022_headcount_trend.sql` (R-P8CN-YQK2): `judgments` gains `headcount_series`, not null with default `[]`, the series as a JSON array; `trend`, not null with default `unknown`; `trend_kind`, not null with default empty; and `trend_from_year`, `trend_to_year`, `trend_from`, `trend_to` and `trend_change`, integers, NULL when the trend is unknown. Nothing is dropped: `assessments` keeps every column and `enrichments_source` stays. This release writes `judgments`, whose `calculated` holds `Calculated`, and every `facts` column, `prospect_lists`, `rejects.prospect_list` with the empty string in `rejects.cohort`, `campaign_uses.prospect_list`, and `passes.judged`, which holds `Judged`, and `passes.scraper`, with 0 in `passes.rescored`, and `links.ein_key` and `link_name_keys` beside every column `links` had, `name_key` among them; it reads none of the columns kept for the previous release. Times are RFC 3339 UTC to the second; an empty time is one not set. SQL keywords used as column names (`trigger`, `rows`, `new`, `keys` and the like) are quoted. The first seven files' text is stated by no requirement, since a test proves the records through the store; the eighth's to the twenty-first's are, because they run on databases a release already filled, so their shape binds the previous release and the next, and the tests write rows as the previous releases would (R-YM0U-JYDQ, R-Z5J8-OA8U, R-YZFQ-RFJD, R-YTC8-UKTW, R-WMLZ-K42F, R-PZSZ-17XK, R-YWZX-ZW1Z, R-AQP9-PPHB, R-ARX6-3H80, R-H7T9-KAD1, R-XNWT-NJR8, R-T5GZ-ZCJ9, R-KV14-69AF, R-ZSPB-XXC1, R-378O-27YS, R-38GK-FZPH, R-3JJQ-52J4, R-3KRM-IU9T, R-LTWP-4SF8, R-LV4L-IK5X, R-EG46-EL1E, R-EHC2-SCS3). `0011` was run, before it was written here, on a scratch copy of the module through appkit's `db.Open` (SQLite 3.53.4): over a database `0001` to `0010` made and into which a row of `assessments` was written naming `fleet` and the verdict's columns, as the previous release writes it, and a pass naming `0010`'s columns, it opened without error and left that row reading `website` `unknown`, `website_url` empty and its verdict kept, the pass's five new counts 0, and `enrichments` with its two indexes. `0013` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a `passes` and a `facts` table holding one row each: it ran without error and left the pass reading `target` NULL and `runs` 0 and the facts row `site_text_at` empty, and a lookup of a candidate's newest copy of a URL planned as `SEARCH pages USING COVERING INDEX pages_candidate (candidate=? AND url=?)`. `0014` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a `facts` table made as `0012` and `0013` make it and holding one row: it ran without error and left that row reading `brief` and `brief_at` empty; afterwards an insert naming exactly `0013`'s columns succeeded and read `brief` empty, and an upsert naming only those columns, of a row given a `brief` and a `brief_at` after the migration, succeeded and left both as they were. `0015` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a `facts` table made as `0012` to `0014` make it and holding four rows whose `website` was `https`, `http`, `none` and `unknown`: it ran without error and left the first two reading `website_source` `check` and the others empty, every `website_search_at` empty; afterwards an upsert naming `0012`'s columns, `site_text_at`, `brief`, `brief_at` and `website_search_at`, in its insert and its update, as release-50's store writes one, of a row whose `website_source` had been set to `search`, succeeded, wrote `website_search_at` and left `website_source` as it was, and an insert of a new row whose `website` was `https` read `website_source` empty. `0016` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over an empty database: it ran without error, a row inserted naming only `candidate` and `campaign` read every other column empty, and a lookup of one candidate's uses planned as `SEARCH campaign_uses USING PRIMARY KEY (candidate=?)`. `0018` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a database the real `0001` to `0017` files made, with two `facts` rows, one with `minutes` 42 and one with `minutes` NULL: it ran without error and left the first reading `minutes_source` `zip` and the second empty, `miles` NULL and `route_address` and `drive_route_at` empty in both; afterwards, a row having been given the source `route`, miles and a route address as this release writes them, an upsert naming `0012`'s columns, `site_text_at`, `brief`, `brief_at`, `website_source`, `website_search_at` and `drive_route_at`, as the previous release writes one, of that row with ZIP minutes succeeded and left the source, miles and address as they were beside the new minutes, and an insert naming those columns but `drive_route_at` read an empty source. `0019` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a database the real `0001` to `0018` files made: it ran without error, two rows inserted naming every column but `seq`, `passing` among them, read `seq` 1 and 2, and a lookup of one list's newest open planned as `SEARCH opens USING INDEX opens_prospect_list (prospect_list=?)`. `0020` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a database the real `0001` to `0019` files made, holding one `facts` row and one `judgments` row: it ran without error and left the facts row reading `headcounts` and `brief_labels` `[]` and the judgments row `headcount_low` and `headcount_high` NULL and `headcount_basis` empty; afterwards, those columns having been given values, an upsert into each table naming only its earlier columns, in its insert and its update, as the previous release writes one, succeeded and left them as they were, and an insert of a new row naming only earlier columns read `[]`, NULL and empty. `0021` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a database the real `0001` to `0020` files made, holding one candidate and one link written naming only `0002`'s columns: it ran without error and left that link reading `ein_key` empty and `link_name_keys` empty; afterwards, that link having been given an `ein_key` and two side rows, an insert of a new link naming only `0002`'s columns and an update of the first setting only `0002`'s columns, as the previous release writes them, succeeded and left the new link's `ein_key` empty and the first link's `ein_key` and side rows as they were; and a lookup of the active candidates by EIN key planned as `SEARCH l USING INDEX links_ein (ein_key=?)` then the candidates by id, and one by name key, state and ZIP as `SEARCH k USING INDEX link_name_keys_key (name_key=?)`, then `links` by its unique `scraper` and `source_id` and the candidates by id. `0022` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a database the real `0001` to `0021` files made, holding one `judgments` row: it ran without error and left that row reading `headcount_series` `[]`, `trend` `unknown`, `trend_kind` empty and the five trend integers NULL; afterwards, those columns having been given values, an upsert naming only the columns of the first twenty-one files, in its insert and its update, as the previous release writes one, succeeded and left them as they were, and an insert of a new row naming only those columns read `[]`, `unknown`, empty and NULL. `0023` was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a database the real `0001` to `0022` files made: it ran without error and left `park_rules` holding one row, 600, 3, 1000000 and an empty run, and `park_labels` thirteen rows; afterwards an insert into `passes` naming only the columns of the first twenty-two files, as the previous release writes one, succeeded and read `parcel_run` empty and `parks_grown` 0. The sandbox check, which sets a pass to `running` by hand and seeds judgments with `sqlite3`, writes these columns.

- `0026_fact_sources.sql` (R-R0AE-AZTH): `candidates` gains `name_source`, `name_record`, `address_source`, `address_record`, `phone_source`, `phone_record`, `email_source` and `email_record`, each not null with default empty, and `other_names`, `other_addresses`, `other_phones` and `other_emails`, each a JSON array, not null with default `[]`, each element an object with the members `Value`, `Source` and `Key`, an address's `Value` an object with the members `Street`, `City`, `State`, `Zip` and `County`; `facts` gains `known_since_source`, `newest_filing_source` and `website_checked_url`, each not null with default empty. `people` keeps its column and its shape, a JSON array of objects whose members `Name` and `Role` the previous release reads, each object gaining the members `Source` and `Key`, which that release's decoding into `sources.Person` ignores; a person the previous release wrote, with neither, reads with both empty. It was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a `candidates` and a `facts` table holding one row each: it ran without error and left the candidate reading the eight new text columns empty and the four arrays `[]` and the facts row its three new columns empty; afterwards an insert naming only the earlier columns read the defaults, and an update setting only an earlier column left a new column set beforehand as it was (R-R1IA-ORK6, R-R2Q7-2JAV).
- `0027_page_reasons.sql` (R-ZYYT-KILO): `pages` gains `reason`, a nullable text column with no default, NULL reading as readable, so a row the previous release inserts, naming none of it, reads as readable, and a row it moves by candidate keeps its reason; `page_refetches` holds one row per entry, `seq` its primary key and the page's number, and `outcome` NULL while pending; the migration's insert fills it with the `seq` of every `pages` row whose `size` is 262144 and whose `seq` is the greatest among the rows of its `candidate` and `url`, by the same grouping `Pages` reads, so an older copy at the old limit whose newer copy read less is not on it. It was run, before it was written here, with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1 over a database the real `0001` to `0026` files made, holding seven pages, three of which were the newest copy of their candidate and URL at 262144, one an older copy at 262144 under a newer smaller one, and one at 262145: it ran without error, left every page's `reason` NULL and put exactly the three in `page_refetches` with `outcome` NULL; afterwards an insert into `pages` naming only `0013`'s columns but `seq`, as the previous release writes one, succeeded with `reason` NULL and added no entry, an update setting only `candidate` left a row's `reason` as it was, and a select of `0013`'s columns of a row whose `reason` was set answered its stored text (R-01EM-C232, R-02MI-PTTR). SQLite documents that a column `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` adds with no `DEFAULT` holds NULL in every existing row (sqlite.org, lang_altertable).
- `0017_prospect_lists.sql` (R-ZNTQ-EUD9): `prospect_lists` with `cohorts`' columns, filled from every `cohorts` row, its id `pl_` and the old id's 16 hexadecimal digits, with the window index `prospect_lists_window`; `rejects` gains `prospect_list`, filled with the mapped id where `cohort` is a `coh_` id, and the index `rejects_prospect_list`; `campaign_uses` gains `prospect_list`, filled the same way, an empty `cohort` staying empty; `judgments` with the judgment columns of `assessments` and `calculated` for `scored`, filled from every `assessments` row, with the index `judgments_operating`; `passes` gains `judged`, set to `rescored`, and `scraper`, empty; and the passes' `rebuild` kind, origin and trigger become `ingest`, the `enrich_cohort` kind `enrich_target`, and a `coh_` target its `pl_` id. Every column added is not null with a default, so an insert the previous release makes, naming none of them, succeeds; the two old not-null columns with no default that this release's inserts still meet, `rejects.cohort` and `passes.rescored`, are written the empty string and 0. The prefix test is `substr(x,1,4) = 'coh_'`, never `LIKE 'coh_%'`, whose `_` matches any byte.
- **`0010` is the contract `0009` promised, and an expand for the verdict** (cohorts decisions 14, 16 and 24). The previous release names none of the dropped columns in what it writes or reads (log 9), so it ran on the database `0010` migrated: its judgments upsert left `fleet_verdict` at its default, so a row it inserted read `unknown` with no counts until the next judge, and a row it updated keeps the verdict this release wrote beside the `fleet` it wrote, which the next judge after a roll forward rewrites; it never reads `prospect_lists` or `rejects`. `fleet_verdict` is not null with a default rather than the nullable column the decisions document names (log 9): the store reads no NULL into a string, and the default is what an unwritten verdict means; it is never derived from `fleet`. The release before the previous one names `confidence` and `base` and does not run on `0010`, which the root rule does not ask. `0011` drops `fleet`, `low` and `high`, as this promised; `candidate_cargo` stays. `0009` and `0010` have run on `us` and are frozen; R-35WR-U8HP and R-TIPT-NOAU stand.
- **`0009` departs from expand then contract** (orchestrator decision 2; the root `AGENTS.md` asks that the release before the current one run on the current one's schema). It kept every column the release before it named, but made `confidence` and `base` nullable and left them NULL, so that release could not read judgments `0009`'s release wrote; a rollback across `0009` was not supported short of a full ingest on that release's own terms, and the orchestrator chose NULL over `DEFAULT 0`, since nothing unknown is stored as 0 (judgments decision 2). Old judgments were discarded, not copied, since the rules change needed a full ingest. `shape/` has no entry on migrations, so this departs from no `shape/` convention; it is recorded here against the root rule. A database a release before `0009` filled still migrates through all eleven files (R-YM0U-JYDQ).
- **`0011` departs from expand then contract** (website decision 22, the owner's decision; the root `AGENTS.md` asks that the release before the current one run on the current one's schema, so an old and new mix works both ways). The previous release writes `fleet` in its judgments upsert, and `0011` drops that column, so the previous release fails on the migrated database at its first judgments write, a judge's or a ruling's: a rollback to it is not supported, short of restoring the database `state/` held before the migration. The owner accepted that risk rather than spend a release carrying `fleet` once more, since the contract `0010` promised was due and nothing reads the column. Nothing else in `0011` breaks the previous release: it never reads `website`, `website_url` or `enrichments`, a pass it records leaves the five new counts at 0, and it reads no column `0011` drops but `fleet`, which it only writes. The requirement that a row the previous release inserts after a rollback reads with an unknown verdict is gone with the rollback it proved. `shape/` has no entry on migrations, so this departs from no `shape/` convention; it is recorded here against the root rule.
- **`0012` is an expand** (enrichers decisions 7 and 14; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds this time): it adds a column with a default, an index, a table and a count, and drops nothing, so the previous release runs on it both ways. That release names `enrichments`' columns in its insert, so a row it adds after `0012` has empty inputs, which differs from any inputs named now, and its candidate is due once more for the website check at the next enrich of the new release (R-WMLZ-K42F): a result recorded on a rollback is refetched once, never lost. It writes no facts row and no `derived`, both left at their defaults, and still writes its facts into the judgments columns, which the new release no longer reads for an active candidate. A candidate whose email domain changed since its last check under the previous release is due after `0012`, its newest result's inputs being the old domain, and is fetched again, which is the inputs rule working.
- **Facts read unknown until the first enrich after `0012`** (decided for this delivery): the migration writes no facts row, and `FillFacts` builds rows only from results the candidate holds, so after deploy only the website facts are back at once, from the website results `0011` kept; the link facts and drive minutes read unknown until the first enrich, which derives both for every active candidate with links or a ZIP, and its follow-on judge end. Seeding the facts rows from the judgments was rejected, since a facts row must be re-made from the results alone (enrichers decision 5, enrichers settled 9); `D11-on-a-space` runs that enrich at once after deploy and compares the prospect lists only after it.
- **`0013` is a plain expand** (site text decision 20; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds a table, an index and columns, each nullable or with a default, and drops and rewrites nothing, so the previous release runs on it both ways and no departure is recorded. That release names its own columns in every insert, so a pass it records reads with an empty target and enrich kind and the four counts 0, and a facts row it writes reads no `site_text` time (R-YWZX-ZW1Z); its facts upsert names its own columns and leaves `site_text_at` as this release wrote it. It never reads `pages`. After a rollback it meets this release's rows too: a `site_text` result is a row of a kind its registry lacks, which its enrich never walks and its candidate page shows as a found object's members, as it shows a derived kind's; an `enrich_target` pass is one of a kind it never starts, shown by its kind word and, failed, by its reason as stored; and a running `enrich_target` is, by its own rule, a pass of a kind other than `enrich`, so it blocks that release's ingest, judge and full ingest until that release's start finishes it `interrupted`, which only a mixed restart meets and which is accepted.
- **`0014` is a plain expand** (brief decision 13; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds two columns to `facts`, each not null with a default, and drops and rewrites nothing, so the previous release, release-46, runs on it both ways and no departure is recorded. That release names its own columns in every `facts` insert and upsert, so a row it inserts reads an empty `brief` and no `brief` time (R-ARX6-3H80), and its upsert of a row this release wrote leaves `brief` and `brief_at` as this release wrote them. After a rollback two consequences are accepted. Its own re-projection of a candidate rewrites `categories` from `link_facts` alone, so that candidate's brief categories drop out of its facts row, and out of the prospect list filters, until this release next writes that row (an enrichment result recorded on the candidate, or a merge or a split touching it; `FillFacts` touches no row already written), while its `brief` and `brief_at` stay as this release left them. And a `brief` result is a row of a kind its registry lacks, which its enrich never walks and its candidate page shows as a found object's members, as release-44 shows a `site_text` result after a rollback across `0013`.
- **`0015` is an expand with a backfill** (website search decision 14, S4; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds two columns to `facts`, each not null with a default, and sets `website_source` to `check` on every row holding a site. The backfill is exact, not a guess: before `0015` only the website check set a row's site, so every such row was decided by the check, and the backfilled row was the facts projection its results gave under that release's website rule, so that release's `FillFacts`, which touched no row already written, need not rewrite it and no full ingest was needed after that deploy. That held under that rule only: under the website states rule a check's `none` no longer decides, and the full `ingest` after that release's deploy brings such rows to it (Recorded decisions). It drops nothing, and the previous release, release-50, runs on it both ways: it never reads `website_source`; its facts write is an upsert naming its own columns and, beside them, the `_at` column of every kind among the candidate's results, so it writes `website_search_at` as this release would and leaves `website_source` as it was (R-XNWT-NJR8), and a row it inserts reads an empty source. After a rollback three consequences are accepted, recorded under Known limits.
- **`0016` is a plain expand** (the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds one table and changes, drops and rewrites nothing, so the previous release runs on it both ways and no departure is recorded. That release names no column of `campaign_uses` and never reads it, so every write and read it makes is the same on the migrated database as before; a database it filled reads, after `0016`, every candidate with no use (R-T5GZ-ZCJ9), since the history begins with the first campaigns event this release takes. What a rollback costs is under Known limits.

- **`0017` is an expand with copies** (funnel rename decisions 5 and 17; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds tables and columns and copies into them, changes no column or table the previous release reads, and rewrites only pass values that release handles, so the previous release runs on it both ways. A probe through appkit's `db` at the previous release's appkit and at this one's showed a database carrying a migration the binary does not know opening with a nil error and a line on `Stderr` naming the unknown version, and an insert by the old binary into a table that gained a not-null column with a default succeeding; so the previous release opens a database `0017` migrated. The copies are cheap: `assessments`, the one large table copied, 43,053 rows on `us`, copied in 0.16 s with its index in a probe of its shape, well inside the start's budget, and `candidates` and `enrichments` are neither copied nor rewritten. The judgments are copied into a table of their own, holding the judgment columns alone (funnel rename decision 13), rather than kept under the old table's name, so the store names nothing it answers by an old word; the old table stays for a rollback. The pass records are rewritten in place rather than copied: one table keeps the prune, the redelivery check and the busy rule whole, and the previous release handles every rewritten value, storing and answering kind, origin and trigger as strings, matching only `running` passes in its busy rule, failing any it finds running at its start, and reading origin `run` alone for redelivery; its only losses are a starter it does not show for origin `ingest` and the target rows of `enrich_target` on its pass page. The old tables and columns go in a later release (`0018` leaves them, recorded below).
- **`0018` is an expand with a backfill** (drive route outcome 15; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds four columns to `facts`, each nullable or not null with a default, and sets `minutes_source` to `zip` on every row holding minutes. The backfill is exact: before `0018` only `drive_minutes` set a row's minutes, so every such row was decided by the ZIP and is the projection its results give (R-378O-27YS), and no full ingest is needed after deploy. `drive_route_at` is added though no kind of the previous release writes it, because that release's facts upsert names the `_at` column of every kind among a candidate's results (R-XNWT-NJR8), so after a rollback it names `drive_route_at` for a routed candidate and would fail without the column. It drops and rewrites nothing else, so the previous release runs on it both ways; what a rollback costs is under Known limits. `0018` does not drop the tables and columns `0017` kept for a rollback, though the release that no longer reads them has shipped: this change is the drive route alone (drive route outcome 14), so that contract is left to a later migration.
- **`0019` is a plain expand** (basis decision 7; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds one empty table and its index and changes no column, so the previous release runs on it unchanged, never reading `opens`, and its writes leave every open (R-3KRM-IU9T). The stored filters gain a `basis` member only for a list that sets one, `omitempty` as every member, an expand the previous release reads by ignoring it (Known limits). The opens' columns hold the answered members with their bases and the passing set, so `D14-prospect-lists` tells `passes` from `moved_up` exactly, from the record, with no inference (decided by the owner for this delivery).
- **`0020` is a plain expand, backfilled by `FillFacts`** (revenue bands decision 19; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds two columns to `facts` and three to `judgments`, each nullable or not null with a default, and drops and rewrites nothing, so the previous release runs on it both ways (R-LTWP-4SF8, R-LV4L-IK5X); what a rollback costs is under Known limits. Unlike `0018`'s, its backfill is not in the migration: the headcounts come from each candidate's newest `link_facts` result and the labels from its newest good brief, a projection the store makes from the decoded results (R-EH1E-KVAS), which the migration does not repeat in SQL, so the migration leaves both at `[]` and the fill each start walks, the engine's `StartFill` (`D06-passes`) calling `FillFacts` page by page in the background after `READY=1`, writes them from the results each candidate already holds (R-EKP3-Q6IV), as it filled the rows after `0012`. A `link_facts` result recorded before this release carries no headcounts, so the facts read none until the full ingest after deploy re-reads the rows and recomputes `link_facts` (revenue bands decision 20), and the judgments' headcount columns stay NULL until it judges everything.
- **The basis words keep their own order, not byte order** (a departure from the categories' normal form, decided for this delivery): the normal form orders the basis words records, partial, enriched, the order the prospect list page states them in and the order a candidate climbs them, so the stored and answered filter reads as the page shows it; byte order would put `enriched` first.
- **An open is one write that answers a read** (basis decisions 7, 8 and 11): `OpenProspectList` works out the answer and records the open in one write transaction at one clock reading, so the open it records is exactly what it answered and a concurrent open compares against it (R-KTT7-SHJQ); `ReadProspectList` stays a read that records nothing and is the peek. An open's time is the clock in UTC truncated to the second, as a reject's is. Who opened is recorded and answered only by `Opens`; no tool or page exposes it. `Opens` is the one read of them, modelled on `Rejects`, so a test proves what an open records through the store, never by reading the table; no tool or page calls it.
- **A deleted list's opens stay** (basis decision 12), as its rejects do; ids are never reused, so they are never compared again.
- **The drive decision is layered over the facts projection** (decided by the delivery): R-D1TM-II5M, which defines the facts projection, stays as it is, and R-EFTI-73K3 states, beside it and in place of its rule for `Minutes`, the four drive fields from the drive decision. Every requirement naming the projection therefore denotes the layered one without being re-minted, and R-D1TM-II5M's own cases, none of which holds a `drive_route` result, read the same under both, since with no deciding route the minutes are the newest `drive_minutes` result's, as before.
- **The headcounts and brief labels are layered over the facts projection too** (decided for this change, after the drive decision's precedent): R-D1TM-II5M stays as it is, and R-EH1E-KVAS states, beside it and beside R-EFTI-73K3, `Headcounts` and `BriefLabels` and their unknown values, so every requirement naming the projection or a fact's unknown value denotes the layered one without being re-minted, here and in `D10-tools` and `D14-prospect-lists`. R-D1TM-II5M's own cases hold no headcount and no labels and read the same under both, each answering the two as slices of length zero.
- **`FillFacts` re-projects every row's headcounts and brief labels at every start** (decided for this change; revenue bands decision 19 asks only for the first start after `0020`): a row written before `0020` cannot be told from one whose results give none, since both hold `[]`, and a row the previous release rewrote after a rollback holds this release's older values, so `FillFacts` sets the two from the results on every row, which also repairs a rollback (R-EKP3-Q6IV). Like the drive source repair it is a scan the fill makes once each start, in the background after `READY=1`, reading each candidate's newest `link_facts` result and newest good brief, not a cost on the pass path, and it rewrites only rows whose two columns change, so a second start writes nothing.
- **`FillFacts` also repairs a disagreeing drive source** (decided by the delivery, extending the sheet's ruling that it re-projects the routed candidates): after a rollback the previous release can write minutes with no source or clear the minutes beside `zip`, on candidates holding no `drive_route` result, which re-projecting the routed candidates alone would leave wrong. A row whose minutes and source disagree is a state no projection gives, so `FillFacts` re-projects it too. This is a scan the fill makes once each start, in the background after `READY=1`, not a cost on the pass path.
- **The fill is walked after `READY=1`, in the background** (decided for this change, after release-75's start was killed by systemd's 90 s start timeout before `READY=1`: on about 90,000 candidates and 480,000 links the start's walk of every `FillFacts` page took longer): `FillFacts` itself is unchanged, its pages, cursor, repairs and idempotence as above (R-EKP3-Q6IV); only who walks it and when moved. The engine's `StartFill` (`D06-passes`) walks every page after the ZIP load, the recovery and the prune, and the start sends `READY=1` without waiting for it (`D03-serve`). While it runs every pass is refused `busy`, so no pass writes a facts row or a display beside the fill; the pages, the tools and the prospect lists keep reading, and a candidate's facts row and display read as the previous start left them until the fill reaches its page, each page landing whole in its one write. A fill the database fails is logged and ends, the service serving on; one cut off by the stop is completed by the next start's, since `FillFacts` is idempotent. Rejected: keeping the walk before `READY=1` under a longer start timeout, since the walk grows with the space and no timeout bounds it.
- **The origin rides on the store's `Config`, not the enrichers'** (decided by the delivery): the origin is part of the subject, since it is part of `drive_route`'s inputs, and every subject is built by the store, in `Subject`, `DueAmong`, `DueEnrichments`, the facts projection's drive decision and a ruling's re-run; the registry the store consults for inputs and validity is `enrichers.New(enrichers.Config{})`, which carries no setting, so the store is the one place every subject passes through. Because the facts row depends on it, a changed origin re-projects the routed candidates' rows at the start (R-EKP3-Q6IV); no other row can hold a route, so no other row changes.
- A link keeps the source's whole reading of its row as JSON beside the key columns matching reads, so a judge and a merge need no read of scrapes and a full ingest rewrites both at once (decision 6, "matching runs against the links and never calls scrapes"). A link an earlier release stored holds a reading without the fields this release added, the fleet verdict and counts among them; it reads with them empty, and a full ingest rewrites it.
- Estimates are answered as `sources.Estimate`, the type a source makes them in; the store adds no type of its own for them, since the candidate is the argument of the read.
- **Kind and origin are two fields** (orchestrator decision 5): a kind says what a pass does and an origin what started it, so a page can show both and a full ingest, one pass that ingests and judges, has kind and origin both `ingest`. The pass kinds are spelled `KindIngest`, `KindJudge`, `KindIngest` and `KindEnrich` beside the ruling kinds, since a name holding `Pass` with a SQL statement's value trips gate 5's `gosec` G101; the reject kinds `KindReject` and `KindUnreject` sit beside them too. `OriginPass` holds `Pass` and passes G101, as `PassIDPrefix` does, its value being one short word (gate 5 run over a scratch copy of the store with the four new constants added: no finding). An enrich the tool starts has origin and trigger `enrich`; a pass another pass starts, an enrich after an ingest or a judge after an enrich, has origin `pass` and the starting pass's id as its trigger, so its record says which pass started it, has no user and no delivery, and is told from a full ingest's or a tool's by its origin (website log A1). A judge started by a pass is not a delivery, so it is not refused as a redelivery, and the trigger refuses only run ids. `KindEnrichTarget` sits beside the pass kinds and `ReasonPrompts` beside the reasons; neither name holds `Pass`. An `enrich_target` has origin and trigger `enrich`, as the tool's corpus enrich has, and is told from it by its kind and its target (site text decision 14).
- **One pass per kind and scope** (site text decisions 15 and 16) is refused by `AddPass` itself, in its write, so two full ingest calls racing cannot both record a pass, nor two enrich calls two enriches, nor two targeted enriches over one target two passes; the slots that kept one ingest, judge or full ingest at a time and one enrich beside it are gone. The scope is a definition in the requirements and not an exported function, since no consumer computes it: the engine learns busy from `AddPass`. Two `enrich_target` passes over one target are busy whatever kinds they walk, site text decision 15 read literally (settled for this delivery, S10); a candidate and a prospect list holding it are two targets, so a pass over each may run at once and both may run the kind for that candidate, an overlap decision 16 accepts as it accepts every other. The delivery is a column of `passes`, not a table of its own, and is forgotten with its pass at the prune, long after the events app's retries end.
- **The busy rule protects no data** (site text decision 16): it stops paid or heavy work being started twice, two website checks over the corpus, two `site_text` passes over one prospect list, two full ingests, and nothing else. appkit's one writer makes the writes behave as one after another (R-W2NM-7T0P); a pass that reads a candidate another pass is half way through writing records what it read, and the next pass or the daily judge corrects it. Release-44 accepted that for an enrich beside the other kinds (enrichers decision 11: a derived kind that reads a candidate an ingest is half way through records inputs of that moment, which the ingest's follow-on enrich finds changed, `D06-passes`); this release accepts it for every pass, so a judge beside an ingest may judge a candidate on half a run's rows and a full ingest beside an ingest redoes that run's rows.
- **Pages are listed whole** (a departure from `shape/`'s Listing, which pages a list an agent can add to): `Pages` answers a candidate's newest copy of each URL without a window, and the `candidate` tool and the candidate page list it whole (`D10-tools`, `D08-pages`). `PageCap` bounds it at 20 per candidate, as configuration bounds the lists that rule lists whole, and more only on a survivor after a merge; copies are never listed. Paging twenty rows would add a token to every candidate answer for nothing.
- **The cap is not re-checked on a merge** (decided for this delivery): only `AddPage` enforces `PageCap`. A merge moves every page of the other candidate to the survivor whatever the survivor holds, so a survivor may hold more than 20 distinct URLs; it then refuses every URL it does not hold and still accepts a newer copy of one it holds. Refusing or trimming a merge for its pages would make the owner's ruling wait on an agent's crawl, and pages are never deleted (site text decision 1).
- **A page's number is global and the database's** (decided for this delivery): it is `pages`' `seq`, so it names one copy among every candidate's, survives a merge unchanged, keeps its text page's path valid on the survivor and is never reused, since no page is removed. That a new page's number is greater than every number before it is required (R-ZP7M-ICO4); its exact value is not.
- **A targeted walk skips, never refuses** (decided for this delivery): `DueAmong` and `DeriveAmong` pass over an id the store does not hold, a merged candidate's and a repeated one, since the engine fixes the targets when the pass is recorded (`D06-passes`) and a ruling may merge one before the walk reaches it; the engine refuses a merged or unknown target before it records the pass. A repeated id is walked once, so a prospect list read cannot make one company paid for twice.
- **Pages are read into a subject only for a `Prompter` kind** (P1, brief decision 3; P1's flag option, keyed on the kind rather than the pass): the subject is defined with its pages and facts always (R-M10O-KUCX, R-UADJ-LEIU, R-CZDT-QYO8, `D16-enrichers`), and `Subject`, `DueAmong` and `DueEnrichments` answer by that definition; no exported method or flag is added. Inside the store the one builder of a subject reads the candidate's pages only when the kind it builds for is an `enrichers.Prompter`: always in `Subject`, which only a prompts kind calls, and in `DueAmong` and `DueEnrichments` when `Get(kind)` of the registry `enrichers.New(enrichers.Config{})` returns a `Prompter`; never in `Derive`, `DeriveAmong` or a ruling's re-run of the derived kinds, nor in `DueEnrichments` or `DueAmong` of a kind that is not a `Prompter`. The builder knows the kind it builds for and not the pass it serves, so the kind is the key; only a targeted pass runs a `Prompter` (release-45's rule, `D06-passes`), so an untargeted walk never reads a page, which is what P1 asks. No `Deriver` and no `Fetcher` reads `Pages`, so a subject built without them gives every such kind the inputs and the results the defined subject gives: the skip cannot be observed by use, carries no requirement, and is recorded here alone. A separate pages-less method was rejected, since a `Prompter`'s due set and inputs must see the pages wherever they are computed, in `DueAmong` and in `DueEnrichments` alike, or a candidate's being due (R-OO40-CCZA) would be false for `brief` on one path; a lazy accessor, P1's other fallback, is not needed, since the kind is known before the subject is built. The facts on the subject are always read, since the builder already reads the facts row for the subject's `Website` and `WebsiteSource`. The subject's `Check`, its newest website check whose inputs share a URL with its current inputs, its Places URL and its email's domain, is read on the same terms as the pages, only for a `Prompter` kind (S5): only `website_search` reads it, into its run's input, and no kind's inputs depend on it, so skipping it elsewhere cannot be observed by use either.
