The subject's `SiteText` and `SearchInputs` (R-EDDP-FK2P) are read on the same terms, only for a `Prompter` kind (empty sites decision 2; `D16-enrichers`): only `website_search` reads them, into its inputs and its run's input, and it is a `Prompter`, so skipping them for every other kind cannot be observed by use either, and they are read in `Subject`, `DueAmong` and `DueEnrichments` alike, so its due set is the same on every path.
- A redelivery of `scrape.finished` is told by the delivery and also by the trigger: an ingest pass already naming the run is enough, so a second `scrape.finished` for one run, under another bus event, is skipped (decision 11). A judge's trigger is the same slug every day, so a judge pass is told by its delivery alone and the same trigger may stand on many passes (orchestrator decision 5).
- A failed pass keeps its counts at 0, as a failed scrapes run does; what it wrote stays in the candidates and links, which the next pass or a full ingest repairs.
- Every id is minted by the store, 8 bytes at a time, at most 8 attempts, as in scrapes; a pass id, a candidate id and a prospect list id are never compared, since their prefixes differ.
- **Judgments are written by a judge, facts by an enrichment** (judgments decisions 6 and 23 to 25, enrichers decision 6): `ReadProspectList` reads the stored judgments, `operating_key`, `revenue_low` and the rest, from the judgments and the facts, `minutes`, `fleet_verdict` and the rest, from the facts rows, and reads the clock only for the filing age and the campaign age (`D14-prospect-lists`), so the tools and pages show the stored numbers, the judgments at most a day old since the daily judge rewrites them and the facts as the last enrichment wrote them. Nothing rewrites a judgment in SQL: the formulas live once, in Go (`D13-judging`). The drive-time filter reads the facts row's `minutes`, which `drive_minutes` recorded from `zip_drive`, so a prospect list read joins no ZIP table. The query joins `facts` by a left join on the candidate, so a member with no facts row reads every fact unknown (NULL, `unknown` or empty) and is admitted or ruled out by the unknown switches exactly as before; `excluded`, the operating and revenue judgments and their keys come from `judgments`, and the class and status from `candidates` (`D14-prospect-lists`' terms read a member's `Judgments` and `Facts`).
- **The operating order is an integer key.** Operating confidence is a float, so the store keeps `operating_key`, the confidence times 1,000,000 rounded to an integer, and -1 for none (`D13-judging` defines it). The operating confidence is stored and answered rounded to six decimal places and the key is derived from that rounded value, so the key is exact and two confidences share a key only when they are equal (orchestrator ruling, after the sandbox showed one 0.2 piece answering 0.19999999999999996; `D13-judging`). The column is not null with default -1, so a row the previous release inserts reads as no confidence; a prospect list's order puts an unknown last by the confidence being nil, not by the key (`D14-prospect-lists`), and the key stays the comparison among known ones.
- **A prospect list keeps the normal form, and an edit compares it** (log 8, log 16): the filters and the order are stored as the JSON of the store's own types, `omitempty` on every filter member, so the zero filter set is `{}`, a `government` of false is kept and a nil one dropped, and the answer encodes the same type, so the stored text is the answered one. An empty categories list reads back as none, which `D14-prospect-lists` treats as the filter not carried unless `category_unknown` is set, when it admits only candidates with no category. An edit builds the prospect list that would result, in normal form, and writes only when its name, size, filters or order differ from the stored ones, so a repeated edit restamps nothing and `changed` says when the definition last changed. Create stamps changed as created, so every column is not null (log 24).
- **A deleted prospect list is removed, not flagged** (cohorts decision 7): no column of log 16 is a deleted flag, the landing lists only prospect lists that exist, and the candidate page shows a record's prospect list as deleted by its missing name. Its reject records stay and keep its id, which minting never reuses.
- **One `Reject` method takes the kind**, as `Decide` takes a ruling's kind, since a reject and an unreject name the same three things and differ in what they record. `Decide` is unchanged and still takes `KindExclude` and `KindInclude`; that `decide` refuses them is the tool's (`D10-tools`).
- **The prospect list store reads `Config.Now` itself** (log 3): a prospect list read's filing-age date comes from the store's own clock, read once per `ReadProspectList`, computed in Go and bound, never SQL's `now`, so the tool and the page answer on the day they are called and a test drives the date through the clock it hands in. A prospect list read is one statement for the members, `LIMIT` the size, and one for the count (cohorts decision 25); which statements, and that the store holds no more than the members, cannot be observed by use, carry no requirement of their own, and are the build's.
- A write given a value no consumer sends answers an error matching `ErrUnreachable` and changes nothing, as in scrapes; `ErrInvalid` is kept for the writes whose arguments come from a person: a ruling, a prospect list and a reject (log 7). The `ValidProspectList` functions are exported so the tools refuse `invalid` before the store is read and the store refuses `ErrInvalid` by the same rule, which cannot disagree.
- **Keyed, never whole** (orchestrator ruling): the pass path reads and writes by key on decision 16's indexes, and no index is added for it but `0021_link_keys.sql`'s two, a departure recorded below (the indexes `0008_windows.sql`, `0009_judgments.sql` and `0010_cohorts.sql` add serve the windows, the phone filter and the reject lookups, `0011_enrichments.sql`'s and `0012_facts.sql`'s the enrichment reads, and `0013_site_text.sql`'s the page reads, a subject's pages among them, and the cap; `0014_brief.sql`, `0015_website_search.sql`, `0018_drive_route.sql`, `0020_headcounts.sql` and `0022_headcount_trend.sql` add none, and `0016_campaign_uses.sql`'s primary key serves a candidate's uses; `0021_link_keys.sql`'s `links_ein` and `link_name_keys_key` serve the match's EIN and name-key lookups): the gone-marking's statement runs on the scraper prefix of the links' unique index and its display re-derivation on the links' candidate index, `JudgeAll` on the candidates' index on status, the match by EIN key on `links_ein` and by name key on `link_name_keys_key`, joined to the links by their unique scraper and source id, and on `links_match` for the first key, each joined to the candidates by id, and a ruling on the candidates' id, the links' candidate index and the rulings by candidate, for the candidates it touches alone. Only the time bound of `D06-passes` and the read that answers while a write is open (R-5BN4-KSWD) are required; the statements are the build's.

- **A link's keys are a column and a side table** (form5500 decisions 15 and 23), a departure from the rule above that the pass path adds no index: a row now finds a candidate by EIN key and by any name key a link holds, and decision 16's indexes hold one name key per link and no EIN key, so either lookup would scan `links` and decode every reading, the whole-table read that rule forbids. One EIN key per link fits a column, `links.ein_key`, indexed by `links_ein`. A link's name keys are an ordered list, which no column can index (SQLite indexes no element of a JSON array), so each is a row of `link_name_keys` with its place, indexed by `link_name_keys_key`. The side table is keyed by the link's source key, never by its candidate, so a merge or a split, which moves links between candidates, moves nothing in it; the name-key lookup joins it to `links` by the unique scraper and source id and takes state and ZIP from `links`, so a state or ZIP a later row changes is read from one place. `name_key` and `links_match` keep holding the reading's first key, written as before, so a link the previous release writes, which writes neither `ein_key` nor side rows, is still found by its first key, and the previous release, which matches on `links_match` alone, finds this release's links as it always did. The column and the side table only find candidate links: a link found by either counts only when its decoded reading holds the key looked up, since the keys a link holds are its reading's (`D12-ingest`), so the indexes never make a link hold a key its reading does not.
- **`0021` is a plain expand** (decision 23): a column with a default, two indexes and a new table, nothing rewritten and nothing dropped, so the previous release runs on it unchanged. Its inserts and updates of `links` name only `0002`'s columns, which leave `ein_key` at its default on a link it inserts and as it was on one it updates, and it never reads `link_name_keys` (R-EHC2-SCS3); it reads `links` naming those columns, which `0021` leaves as they were. A database it filled opens under all twenty-one with every link as it was (R-EG46-EL1E). The migration fills nothing: a link written before it holds no EIN key and only its `NameKey`, as its reading, which carries neither an `EINKey` nor `NameKeys`, says, so matching by the new keys finds nothing extra until the backfill, which is safe (decision 21). The backfill is the full ingest under the new rules version (decision 24): its `Attach` calls, with `force` true, rewrite every link's reading, and with it the link's `ein_key` and its side rows, which a write of a link replaces whole. The migration is frozen once it has run on `us`.
- **The writes of the keys carry no requirement of their own**: a link round-trips its whole `Reading`, its `EINKey` and `NameKeys` among it (`D12-ingest`'s `Attach` requirements state each link's `Reading` equal to its observation's), and the column and the side rows exist only to find a link by key, which `D12-ingest`'s matching requirements observe: a row that finds a candidate by an EIN key or a name key a link holds proves the keys were recorded, and one that no longer finds it after a forced `Attach` changed the link's reading proves they were rewritten. A test never reads the tables (candidates/AGENTS.md).
- **`0022` is a plain expand** (headcount trend decisions 13 and 14; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds eight columns to `judgments`, each nullable or not null with a default, and drops and rewrites nothing, so the previous release runs on it both ways (R-P9KK-CIAR, R-E8FQ-9PRL); what a rollback costs is under Known limits. It needs no backfill: the defaults read as no series and an unknown trend (R-P5WV-772O), which is what a candidate this release has not yet judged has, and the judge over everything after deploy writes every candidate's series and trend from the headcounts its facts row already holds, with no full ingest, since no reading or fact changes (`D13-judging`). The trend filter needs no column: it is a member of the filters JSON `prospect_lists` already stores, and the trend order a term of its ordering JSON.
- **`0023` is a plain expand with seed rows** (parks decisions 1 and 2; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): it adds five tables and nine columns of `passes`, each not null with a default, inserts the one rules row and the seed labels, and changes, drops and rewrites nothing, so the previous release runs on it both ways (R-6FVT-78DA, R-6IBL-YRUO); what a rollback costs is under Known limits. The seed is data in the migration, not code, so the rules a fresh store answers are the rows a test reads back (R-64U1-LEO8), and an edit changes only rows.
- **A derivation is applied in one write** (parks decisions 3 and 4): the parks are a few hundred rows over a few thousand parcels, the industrial parcels of about 832,000 rows, so `ApplyParks` writes a whole derivation and ends its pass in one transaction, which the busy retry covers as it covers the paged writes (R-DS8O-A9S0); a failed pass leaves the last applied derivation whole, and a read never sees half of one. Its memory is the plan its caller holds, bounded by the industrial parcels, not by the rows read (`D06-passes`).
- **The park windows carry no plan requirement** (a departure from the candidate, pass and prospect list windows, R-VANB-FF5W and the plan requirements after it): the parks are at most a few hundred, and the minutes sort reads each park's ZIP minutes from `zip_drive` at the read, so a window reads the parks and orders them in its statement; no statement is exported and no index required, and a test proves the windows by their answers alone (R-64WP-RAP1, R-664M-52FQ).
- **A park's name and minutes are read, not stored** (parks decision 4): the name counts the parks of its county and town at the read (`D18-parks`), so a second park minted in a town renames the first with no write, and the minutes follow the ZIP table as a facts row's ZIP minutes do.
- **`SetParkRules` records its pass in its own write** (parks decision 5): the edit and the parks pass it starts land together or not at all, so an edit refused `ErrBusy` leaves the rules as they were and an edit that lands always has its re-derivation recorded. It is not a write unit, as `CreateProspectList` is not: a tool call answers its busy write.
- **The run the parks were last derived from is stored, not read off a pass** (a departure from parks decision 1, recorded in `D18-parks`): `ApplyParks` records it in the write that ends the pass, and `ParksRun` answers it after that pass is pruned (R-W7J7-QVZH).
- **The park words are spelled beside the pass words**: `KindParks`, `OriginRules`, `OriginStart` and `TriggerParkRules` hold no `Pass`, so gate 5's G101 passes them; `OriginRun` is reused for the parks delivery, which a `scrape.finished` starts as it starts an ingest, and the park id's prefix and validator sit beside the other ids' (`D18-parks`).
- **The series is a JSON column, the trend columns of their own** (decided for this change): the series is read only whole, on the card and in the `candidate` tool, so it is kept as one JSON array, as the facts row keeps its headcounts; the trend, its change and its basis are columns, since the prospect list filter compares `trend` and the order sorts on `trend_change` in the one members query (`D14-prospect-lists`), as `revenue_low` and `minutes` are. The trend's words are stored as `D13-judging`'s constants spell them, and `unknown` is a stored word, never NULL or empty, so a filter's `trend_unknown` compares a value like the others.
- **The trend words keep their own order, not byte order** (decided for this change, after the basis words' precedent): the normal form orders them growing, flat, shrinking, the order the prospect list page states them in, so the stored and answered filter reads as the page shows it; byte order would put `flat` first.
- **The OSHA ITA change needs no migration** (OSHA ITA outcomes 16 and 18; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): every datum it adds is a member of JSON a column already stores, so the schema stayed twenty-one migrations and the file list was unchanged. A link's `reading` gains the reading's `Establishment`, `Year`, `Employees` and `Hours` and its headcount's `Hours`; an enrichment's `found` gains a `link_facts` headcount's `hours` (`D16-enrichers`); a facts row's `headcounts` gains each element's `hours`; and an estimate's `facts` gains the judge's headcount's `hours` (`D13-judging`). `judgments.headcount_basis` takes the new word `employees:osha_ita` as the text it already holds. Each member is only added, absent or empty where a source gives none, never renamed or removed, so expand then contract needs no contract step: the previous release decodes every one of these with `json.Unmarshal`, which ignores a member its types lack, and its `LinkFacts.Valid`, which refuses an unknown member, runs only at `AddEnrichments` of the results it derives itself, which carry no `hours`; and this release reads what the previous release writes, which lacks the members, with them empty or nil. What a rollback costs is under Known limits.
- **The hours are layered over the headcounts** (decided for this change, after the headcounts' precedent): R-H6LD-6IMC states the `Hours` of each element of the projection's `Headcounts` beside R-EH1E-KVAS, which stays as it is, so every requirement naming the facts projection denotes the layered one without being re-minted. Re-minting R-EH1E-KVAS instead would re-mint R-H5DG-SQVN and R-EKP3-Q6IV, which cite it, for no change in what they state; R-H5DG-SQVN's description of a headcount, which names no hours, is incomplete for an OSHA ITA headcount, not false, and its type, `sources.Headcount`, gains the field in `D01-layout-and-run-seam`.
- **Reading what the previous release wrote carries no requirement of its own** (decided for this change): a link whose stored reading lacks the four OSHA ITA fields, and a facts row or `link_facts` result whose headcounts lack `hours`, read with them empty or nil because `json.Unmarshal` leaves a missing member at its zero value, which no migration or read path of this release changes. R-EG46-EL1E already states a link's `Reading` as `json.Unmarshal` of its stored reading, R-H6LD-6IMC's cases include a `link_facts` headcount with no `hours` member and a facts row the previous release left without `hours`, and the store writes a nil `Hours` as an absent member, so a requirement for the previous release's rows would restate those.
- **Ruling points 1 and 2 carry no requirement of their own** (orchestrator ruling): reads by key and work confined to a batch's rows and their candidates cannot be observed by use, though a page's bound can (R-EN4W-HQ09), since a test never reads tables or counts statements; the cost requirements of `D06-passes` (R-00FD-PO9J, R-1YYP-WJMZ, R-WRT5-YPNE) are their proof, and the read during a write (R-5BN4-KSWD) is ruling point 4's.
- **Upkeep touches only its own table, and its memory is the bound** (log 36): `ReplaceZipDrive` and `PrunePasses` had gone through a write that read every record of every table into memory, copied it and compared the copy afterwards, so the start took 450 to 700 MB over the `us` space's 43,000 candidates and 54,000 links and was killed under `memory_max = 256M` (`D02-cli`). They were that path's only callers, so the path is gone with them; every write is now bounded by what it touches. Which statements they run is the build's; what use can observe is the memory a call allocates, which a test reads with `runtime.ReadMemStats` around the call. R-R5M9-468R binds it over the store `D06-passes`' cost chain leaves behind, 5,000 active candidates judged and 10,000 links, generated as the time bounds' runs are, so no production data is needed: measured over that store, each call allocated about 174 MiB through the whole-database path, 0.7 MiB for the same call over an empty store, and reading the 5,000 candidates alone allocated 15.5 MiB, so the bound of 4 MiB fails a call that reads the candidates whole, and so any that reads the links, twice as many, and leaves the keyed calls room. The cost store holds no ruling, prospect list or reject, so a call that read one of those tables whole would pass; this limitation is accepted, since those tables grow only by the owner's hand. `TotalAlloc` counts every goroutine of the test process and only grows, so a collection does not disturb it; the test that reads it does not call `t.Parallel`, waits for the pass's ending event in its capture before it measures, and makes no other call while it measures. R-LL7Q-0WU0 binds the prune over a store of its own, 20,000 `done` passes, since the cost store holds too few passes for a whole read to fail the bound; P17 made the prune a page of `n`, so a read of every pass past keeping, about 10 MB as `Pass` values, fails it and a page of 500 passes it.
- **An unchanged row still re-derives its candidate's display once per batch**: `D12-ingest` requires the display of every candidate an `Attach` returns current after it, and a re-read row can clear its link's gone, which orders the display; the cost stays one re-derivation per candidate per batch, from that candidate's own links. The re-derivation is a pure function of the candidate's links and their readings: no order that picks a candidate's facts, or a link among equals, reads a link's last seen, the candidate's `Updated`, attach order or any other value ingest refreshes on a re-read, and ties go to the source key (`Scraper`, then `SourceID`, as `Links` orders them), so re-deriving a candidate none of whose links changed in anything but last seen gives the same facts. Rejected: breaking ties by last seen, the newest record first, which an earlier draft did; ingest refreshes last seen one row at a time, so the chosen record flipped during a pass and the candidate's `Updated` moved though nothing about it changed. This goes beyond the ruling's "one lookup and a touch of last seen", deliberately.
- **A read never waits for a write**: appkit's `db.Read` answers from the last commit while `db.Write` holds the writer, which a probe against appkit's released `db` showed (a read returned while a test held a write transaction open), and the store adds no lock of its own across a transaction. The store this replaces held one store-wide lock across every transaction, so a read waited behind a write that was itself waiting for the writer; a test holding a `Write` open while an `Attach` waits tells the two apart. A lock the store took only inside its own write transaction, after appkit's writer is won, would escape that test, and this limitation is accepted: use cannot observe when an `Attach` is inside its own transaction, and a lock held there blocks reads only for as long as the batch's own write transaction, which the time bound of `D06-passes` keeps short. Likewise the test cannot confirm that its waiting `Attach`, `Decide` and `Judge` have begun waiting before it reads, since the design declares no hook that would tell it: a store lock held across a whole write call is caught only when a waiting call has taken it before the reads end, which the test makes likely, not certain, by starting the writes first and yielding between reads; this limitation is accepted too.
- **The judge is paged by its caller** (P15 (a), P16, P17): `JudgeAll` judges one page per call, the active candidates after the caller's cursor in id order, at most the `n` it is given, in one write, stamped with the judge time `at` the caller passes, and answers the page's last id as the next cursor; the pass walks the pages with `passes.BatchSize` and one `at` read once from its clock, so every candidate a pass judges carries one `Calculated` however many pages it took (`D13-judging`), and its `Judged` and `Unranged` are the pass's sums. P15 first had the store walk its own windows of 500 inside one `JudgeAll`, stating the value as a number since it cannot import `passes`; that made the windows unobservable by use and kept a whole-space walk inside one call, which P17 rules out. The caller-driven page follows `Derive`'s precedent, so the store states no batch size of its own and a test proves the page by use: one call touches at most `n` candidates after its cursor (R-EN4W-HQ09). Each page is short, so the writer is free between pages for litestream's checkpoint and for every other write. The read during a write (R-5BN4-KSWD) now has `JudgeAll` take effect wholly or not at all, like every other write, since one call is one transaction.
- **A busy write is retried in the store, not in the engine** (P15 (b); a departure from scrapes, whose engine retries its run endings through a store interface it takes so that a test can inject a lock): each of candidates' six units is one store call whose transaction the store owns, so a retry in the store needs no store interface on candidates' engine, and the sweep's and the start's calls retry as the pass's do. The cost is the proof: a test meets a real lock, so each busy attempt waits out appkit's busy timeout, which candidates/AGENTS.md admits as the one exception to its waits on a clock, bounded as it states: R-DUOH-1T9E exhausts the retries once, on `Attach` alone, waiting six of them, about thirty seconds, and R-DTGK-O1IP waits one per method, about five seconds each. appkit's `db` (the release candidates requires) publishes no way to shorten it: `db.Config` names the path, the migrations, the clock, the service and `Stderr`, and `db.Open` sets `busy_timeout(5000)` itself. The tests of the six methods each build their own database, so they need not wait in turn.
- **The facts projection reads by kind** (P15 (d), P16): rewriting a facts row read every result the candidate ever had, a set that grows with every forced derive; it now reads, on `enrichments_candidate` (candidate, kind, seq), each kind's newest result, and further back within a kind only as far as its fact's definition needs an older one: the newest good brief, the website decision among the checks and searches, and the deciding route among the `drive_route` results, each folded as read and kept as the decision alone. The facts it projects are the same, so R-D1TM-II5M and the requirements layered over it stay as they are. What use can observe is the memory a projection allocates, so R-Y053-JMWZ binds it as R-R5M9-468R binds the upkeep: a candidate holding 4,000 `link_facts` results of 4 KiB each, 16 MB of `Found`, and every other kind the projection reads padded or multiplied past the bound, its newest brief failed so the good one before it is read too, is projected within 4 MiB by `AddEnrichments`, `Derive` and `FillFacts` alike, which a read of every result fails and a read of the newest per kind passes with room. An exported statement whose plan a test checks, as `ProspectListsAfter`'s is, was rejected: it would prove the statement, not that the projection runs it, and the projection needs more than the newest result of the brief, website and route kinds.
- `ListCandidates`' `phone` filter is matched on the links' phone keys, any link, gone or not, with the filter normalized by `sources.PhoneKey`: a phone is identity evidence, and a gone link still counts for identity (decision 6).
- **Windows by position, not offset** (appkit's D25): each list read takes the position of the last entry of the window before and a count `n`, as prompts' `Runs` does; a position is the sort key of the entry, the created text (`Created` as RFC 3339 UTC, which is how it is stored) and id for a candidate, the started text and id for a pass, the name and id for a prospect list, and an empty id asks for the first window. The tools and pages pass `n` one more than the window `paging.Window` read. An `n` below 1 is refused `ErrUnreachable`, as every value no consumer sends is in candidates' store, where prompts answers an empty window. The landing no longer ranks a window, so no offset count is kept.
- **The order each window takes, and the plan that serves it**: candidates `status = 'active'`, `(created, id) < (?, ?)`, `ORDER BY created DESC, id DESC`, on `candidates_window`; passes `(started, id) < (?, ?)`, `ORDER BY started DESC, id DESC`, on `passes_window`; prospect lists `(name, id) > (?, ?)`, `ORDER BY name, id`, on `prospect_lists_window`, `shape/`'s Storage row value, catalogs by name (log 18). The indexes carry `id` because a table whose primary key is text keeps its rows by rowid, so `candidates_created` and `passes_started`, ordered by rowid among equal times, cannot serve the id tie-break; those two are left in place, and a later contract migration may drop them. **Each window is two fixed statements** (orchestrator decision D28): the first window and the window after a position are separate statements, exported from `internal/store` as `CandidatesFirst` and `CandidatesAfter`, `StartedFirst` and `StartedAfter` for the passes (named for their order, since gate 5's `gosec` G101 flags a string constant whose name holds `pass` as a hardcoded credential), and `ProspectListsFirst` and `ProspectListsAfter`, each with every value bound (as scrapes' rows window and prompts' `Runs` are), and each window method runs the first of its pair for an empty id and the second otherwise: SQLite cannot take a disjunction guarded by a bound value as an index bound, so a folded statement walked its index from the start on every deep page (the sandbox check over 10,000 candidates and 2,000 passes). The filters may stay guarded by their bound values (`?='' OR ...`), since none of them is an index bound; the phone probe plans as a correlated subquery on `links_phone`. A first window has no position to seek to, so the passes' and the prospect lists' are a `SCAN` of their index in its own order, stopped by the `LIMIT`, and the candidates' a `SEARCH` on `status = ?`. Observed through appkit's driver (`Read` on a handle `db.Open` returned, SQLite 3.53.4) and with the `sqlite3` CLI 3.46.1, over the nine migrations for the candidates and passes, as recorded for `0009` (`CandidatesAfter` `SEARCH candidates USING INDEX candidates_window (status=? AND (created,id)<(?,?))`, `CandidatesFirst` the same with `(status=?)`, `StartedAfter` `SEARCH passes USING COVERING INDEX passes_window ((started,id)<(?,?))` for a statement selecting the id alone, `StartedFirst` `SCAN passes USING INDEX passes_window`), and over the ten for the prospect lists (`ProspectListsAfter` `SEARCH prospect_lists USING INDEX prospect_lists_window ((name,id)>(?,?))`, `COVERING INDEX` for a statement selecting the id and name alone, `ProspectListsFirst` `SCAN prospect_lists USING INDEX prospect_lists_window`, before and after `ANALYZE`); no `USE TEMP B-TREE` in any. appkit's driver refuses a statement given fewer arguments than its parameters, so the requirements declare each statement's parameters in order. The plans are required of the six window statements (R-RXX9-K4R5, R-U8UF-KJ6D, R-TRRU-7QSN), and each statement's rows are required to be its method's window (R-XWJK-VR9D). That a window method runs its statement rather than another giving the same rows cannot be observed by use, since no test reads source or traces statements; this limitation is accepted, and the sandbox check's deep-page timings and plans cover it on the real binary.
- **The reversed windows mirror the forward ones** (decided 2026-10-13, for the landing's Prev and Last, `D08-pages`) rather than taking a direction flag: each is two fixed statements, as each forward window is, with every value bound, and each reversed method runs the first of its pair for an empty id and the second otherwise. `ListPassesReversed` runs `StartedLast`, `ORDER BY started ASC, id ASC LIMIT ?`, and `StartedPreceding`, `WHERE (started, id) > (?, ?) ORDER BY started ASC, id ASC LIMIT ?`; `ListProspectListsReversed` runs `ProspectListsLast`, `ORDER BY name DESC, id DESC LIMIT ?`, and `ProspectListsPreceding`, `WHERE (name, id) < (?, ?) ORDER BY name DESC, id DESC LIMIT ?`. They are named for the end they start from and the side of the position they read, mirroring `First` and `After`. Both read the forward windows' indexes in the other direction. Observed as above: `StartedPreceding` `SEARCH passes USING COVERING INDEX passes_window ((started,id)>(?,?))` for a statement selecting the id alone, `StartedLast` `SCAN passes USING INDEX passes_window`, `ProspectListsPreceding` `SEARCH prospect_lists USING INDEX prospect_lists_window ((name,id)<(?,?))`, `ProspectListsLast` `SCAN prospect_lists USING INDEX prospect_lists_window`; no `USE TEMP B-TREE` in any. They answer in the reverse of list order, the order a keyset read from a position backwards takes; the landing page reverses the window it draws. An `n` below 1 is refused `ErrUnreachable`, as the forward windows refuse it. The plans are required of the four (R-PZ73-DFQV, R-NVLB-NNRT) and their rows to be their methods' windows (R-VKEI-HL3G); that a method runs its statement rather than another giving the same rows is accepted unobserved, as for the forward windows.
- **The name filter compares Go's lowering, not SQL's.** `names_lower` is written as Go's `strings.ToLower` of the names joined by line feeds, and SQLite's `lower()` folds only ASCII, so the filter is lowered with `strings.ToLower` before it reaches SQL and found with `instr(names_lower, ?) > 0`, a plain substring test: no `LIKE`, so `%` and `_` need no escape, and no `LIKE` pattern-length limit (50,000 bytes by default) can fail a long filter. The brief's `names_lower LIKE '%' || lower(?) || '%'` with an escape would differ from the Go semantics on non-ASCII letters; this does not. The one difference from the Go semantics the store had is the line feed that joins the names: a filter holding a line feed matches no candidate, where it matched a name holding the same line feed before; no source writes one into a name.
- **What still reads whole tables.** The windows and every read a page or a tool makes read by key or by window: `GetCandidate`, `Links`, `Estimates`, `GetJudgments`, `Rulings`, `Rejects`, `GetProspectList`, `GetPass`, `ZipMinutes`, `Pages`, `GetPage` and `CountPages` read one candidate's, one prospect list's, one pass's or one page's rows by index. `ReadProspectList` reads the judgments of the active candidates its filters do not rule out, ordered by its order and cut by its size, and counts them, which `D14-prospect-lists`' definition asks of it; its standing rejects come from `rejects_prospect_list`. `RunningPasses` is no page's or tool's read: the start calls it once, and with no index on `status` it scans `passes`, whose size is the configured retention plus the passes recorded since the last sweep. Nothing on the pass path or in upkeep reads a set that grows with the space in one call: `JudgeAll`, `MarkGone`, `Derive`, `FillFacts`, `DueEnrichments` and `PrunePasses` each read one page (Bounded work), and `ReplaceZipDrive` reads and writes `zip_drive` alone, whose size is the release's table, configuration rather than data (log 36). The one read left whole is a prospect list's passing set (below); the park reads, `PriorParks` and the park windows among them, read the parks table, whose size is the parks of the space's industrial parcels, a few hundred, not the candidates. `Decide` reads and writes by key the candidates it touches alone (orchestrator ruling, after the sandbox found one ruling taking eight minutes over 43,058 candidates and 53,787 links, past the MCP gateway's 50 seconds; `D12-ingest`'s requirements bind it). The engine's redelivery check (`D06-passes`, R-YRFA-2BM8) does not list the passes: `FindDelivered` asks for one pass carrying the delivery's event id or naming its run, on `passes_delivery` and on `passes_trigger`, which `0008` adds; for a judge delivery the engine passes no run, so only the delivery arm can match. `passes_delivery` is partial (`WHERE delivery <> ''`, `0006`), so the delivery arm states `delivery <> ''` for SQLite to use it; and since two passes can match at once (one by delivery, another by run), the first in pass order is chosen with `ORDER BY started DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1`. With the guard, `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` on SQLite 3.46.1 shows the two arms as `SEARCH passes USING INDEX passes_delivery (delivery=?)` and `SEARCH passes USING INDEX passes_trigger (trigger=?)`. That a list or page read holds no more than its window cannot be observed by use, since a test never counts statements or reads tables (the tests of R-XWJK-VR9D and the plan requirements run only the statements the store exports, never SQL of their own); it carries no requirement of its own, and the sandbox check measures it.
- **`candidate_cargo` is still written, and observed by nothing** (log 30): every write of a candidate's `cargo` replaces that candidate's `candidate_cargo` rows in the same transaction, so a release that still filters on cargo reads it right after a rollback; `JudgeAll` no longer re-derives the table, the repair having served only the interim ranked list (`D13-judging`). Cargo is not a prospect list filter (cohorts decision 4), so no read of this release reads the table, and the requirements only that list could observe are gone with it; the upkeep is carried by this prose alone, since a test may not read tables. Dropping the table is a finding for the next contract.
- **The `fleet`, `low` and `high` columns are gone** (website decision 22): `0011` drops them, and the store writes none of them; their absence after the migration is proved by `0011`'s bytes (R-J19N-RJ4G), since no test reads tables, and checked in the sandbox.
- **Enrichment results are generic** (enrichers decision 4): `store.Enrichment` carries the kind, the inputs, the source, what was found as raw JSON, the cost and the time, one shape for every kind, and the registry's `Valid` is what the store checks a found against (`D16-enrichers`); the website check's fields left the store type and are decoded from `Found` by the consumers that show them into a `website.Result`. The website found stays `json.Marshal` of a `website.Result`, members spelled as the Go fields, since the previous release decodes `found` into its own `store.Enrichment` by those names; the derived kinds' members are snake_case and none equals one of that type's field names in any case, so the previous release decodes a candidate's derived rows without failing (`D16-enrichers`). `Cost` cannot be negative, NaN or infinite and `Inputs` cannot be empty; anything else is a value no consumer sends.
- **The facts row is the newest result of each kind** (enrichers decision 5): each kind sets its own columns and its own `_at` from its newest result, and a kind with no result leaves its columns unknown. A candidate whose inputs for a kind become empty (its links all gone from the store, its ZIP cleared, its domain lost) records nothing new for that kind and keeps the facts of its last result, as `D13-judging` once kept a candidate that stops being checked at its last result; this departs from enrichers decision 3's "the facts projection shows unknown", because a facts row must stay the projection of the results, re-made from them alone (enrichers decision 5, enrichers settled 9). `drive_minutes` records a result with no minutes for a ZIP the table lacks, so a ZIP moving to an unknown one clears the minutes and is not examined again at every pass (`D16-enrichers`). `brief`, the website and the drive minutes are the facts that come from another result than a kind's newest: the brief's text and categories from its newest good brief, recorded next, the website from the website decision, recorded after it, and the minutes from the drive decision, recorded under the facts row.
- **The facts take the newest good brief's categories, not the newest brief result's** (a departure from the letter of brief decision 7, which names the newest `brief` result): a failed run carries no categories (brief decision 6), the text comes from the newest result whose run is not failed (brief decision 8), and the candidate page shows that same result with its categories (P5); reading the categories from the newest result while the text came from an earlier one would set one run's summary beside another's categories, or, after a failure, leave a summary with none of its own. So the row's `brief` and its brief categories always come from one result, the newest good brief (R-D1TM-II5M), and the Verification's "a failed run ... no category from it is added" holds, a failed run leaving both as the last good one left them. `brief_at` still names the newest result, failed or not, as every `_at` does (enrichers settled 8), so a member's brief time moves on a failed run while the text and categories do not.
- **The website is the newest check's site, else the newest confirmed search's, else the newest unfound search's `none`** (website search decision 7, S4, website states outcomes 1 to 3): a check that found a site decides whatever the searches found, but for a takeover search run against that site or against a site a search decided (below), since the search otherwise runs only where no site is on record and a later check that finds the domain serving a site takes over; among the searches the newest confirmed one decides, not the newest search, so a forced search that finds nothing, fails, or names a site that does not load leaves the site an earlier search confirmed standing, as a failed brief leaves the last good one. Where no site was found, the newest search that is not failed and found nothing, naming no URL or one that did not load, decides `none`, with the agent's why, and a failed search or one that named a URL and holds no confirmation decides nothing, so an older unfound search stands. A check's `none` decides nothing (website states outcome 2): a domain that serves no site is a company whose site no one has found yet, so the row reads `unknown` until a search has run; the check still records that result, with its attempts and refresh unchanged (`D15-website-check`). The decision is a function of the results alone, as the newest good brief is, so a full ingest gives the same answer. `force` cannot clear a wrong found site (Known limits). The words are `store.WebsiteByCheck` and `store.WebsiteBySearch`, `check` and `search`, as the facts row stores them and the tools answer them, declared in the store because the store writes them and every other document refers to them by name; an unfound search's `none` has no source word, as no site is there to attribute.
- **A takeover search decides over the check of the site it ran against, and an inert search decides nothing** (empty sites outcomes 5 to 8, empty sites decisions 3 and 17; a departure from website search decision 7, where the check's site always wins): site text showed the check's site empty, and a search that then found another site and loaded it is the better answer, so the newest check yields to any search that is not failed, whose confirmation loaded a page on a host other than that of its recorded website, and whose recorded website is on the host of the URL that check found, or is a site a search had decided, recorded before or after it; a check that later finds a site on another host is a new site on record and decides again, unless the takeover search ran against a site a search had decided (empty sites decision 19, below). Whether the search left that site is read from the two members the result records, the website it ran against and the URL its confirmation landed on, by host, lowercased with one trailing `.` and one leading `www.` removed, the comparison `D16-enrichers` makes for the case, and never from the recorded case: release-72 decided the case from the answer's URL, so a named site that redirected back onto the site on record reads `different` in its results, and one on the same host that redirected elsewhere reads `stands`. A confirmation that loaded the site on record, a `stands` or `none` answer holding no confirmation beside a site on record, and a named site that did not load beside one are left out of the confirmed and the unfound search rather than ranked below them, so the decision is the one the slice gives without them: a `stands` confirmation never moves the URL, title, why or time to its own copy of the same site, and a `none` never turns a found site into `none` (empty sites outcome 7). Each rule reads only the results, newest first, and what each recorded, never the order the takeover search and the check were recorded in, so a rebuild, a full `ingest` or `FillFacts`, gives the same answer. A search recorded before the cases has no website and no case, so it is neither inert nor a takeover search and decides exactly as before, and no migration rewrites a result.
- **A takeover is judged by where the confirmation landed, not by the recorded case** (empty sites decision 17; empty sites outcomes 5 and 6): release-72 judged a takeover by the result's case, which it decided from the answer's URL, so a named site that redirected back onto the site on record, seen live on a Facebook page and a Google sign-in page already on record, read `different` and turned the facts source to `search` at a URL on the host already on record, the same URL or, for the sign-in page, one with a new query token. The decision now reads the result's `website` and its confirmation's `URL` and compares their hosts, as `D16-enrichers` now decides the case, so a confirmation that landed on the site on record is inert whatever case it recorded, and one that landed elsewhere takes over whatever case it recorded. Reading the stored members rather than the case lets a rebuild from results correct the rows release-72 wrote, with no migration and no change to the schema or a payload; the search's website still ties the search to the check it overrides, by host since empty sites decision 19 (below). Rejected: trusting the case, which would leave release-72's rows wrong until each company's next search.
- **A takeover is tied to the check by host, or by a search having decided the site** (empty sites decision 19; empty sites outcome 5): the byte-for-byte match between a search's recorded website and the check's URL missed two takeovers. A company whose site a search took over, its facts source `search` at B while the check found A, may be searched again, reason `again`, and that run's recorded website is B, not A, so when it confirmed a new host C the match failed and the row fell back to the check's A, losing both B and C. And a row whose URL differs from the check's only by a query string, as release-72's confirmation wrote for a Google sign-in page, failed the match although it is the check's site. So a search that is not failed, whose confirmation loaded a page and landed on a host other than its recorded website's, takes over when its recorded website is on the host of the check's URL, under the site key's host rule, or its `website_from` is `search`, the site it ran against being one a search decided; the stands, none and inert rules are unchanged but for comparing by host. The newest such search decides, as the rule already orders results, so a later `again` run that confirmed C decides over the takeover B, and a later `stands` or `none` run, inert, leaves B. `Found`, `Valid`, the case, the run input, the pass counts, the template and the schema are unchanged, and no migration runs: the rebuild from the stored members applies the rule to every row it writes. Rejected: re-recording the check's URL on each search, which would change the run input and still leave the rows already written wrong.
- **The new website reading needs no migration** (website states, decided by the delivery): the facts row's columns, the `sources.Website*` states and the store's source words are unchanged, so the schema is unchanged and expand then contract is met trivially; only the projection changes. Every write that records a result rewrites the whole row by the new rule, and a full ingest's forced `link_facts` walk records one on every active candidate (R-EAXW-O0LB, and R-HGCK-8OJW and R-EOCS-VHQY in `D06-passes`), so one full `ingest` after the deploy brings every row to the new reading; until something rewrites the row, that ingest, the candidate's next result, or the start's `FillFacts` for a candidate holding a `drive_route` result or whose minutes and minutes source disagree (R-EKP3-Q6IV), a row the previous release wrote keeps its old reading (Known limits). The takeover rule of empty sites decision 17 needs none either: it reads the `website` and `confirmation` members every search since release-72 records, so the same rebuild from results, a full `ingest`'s forced walk, the candidate's next result or `FillFacts` where it re-projects, corrects the rows release-72 wrote for a search whose named site redirected back onto the site on record, and release-72, run on this release's database after a rollback, reads those members unchanged by its own rule. A backfill in SQL was rejected, since the reading of a search result lives in Go and a facts row must be re-made from the results alone (enrichers decision 5).
- **The website source is read from the facts row** (website search decision 10): `store.Facts`' `WebsiteSource` is answered at the read from the facts row, as its other facts are, and no judge writes a source. A merged candidate has no facts row and answers no facts; the candidate page shows its website from its results.
- **The brief's set or the link facts', never both** (category evidence decisions 1 to 3 and 11): a good brief with at least one category gives the row's categories alone, each with the source `brief`, and otherwise the row holds the link facts' categories as release-46 built them; a good brief with no category decides nothing, so a company whose website check found the wrong site is not made unknown by it. Every result keeps every category its source gave and only the row's choice changes, so nothing is migrated: the schema and the rules version are unchanged, no column records which set decided, and `store.ClassifiedBy` reads it from the sources in the set. Every write that records a result rewrites the whole row by these rules, so a full ingest's forced `link_facts` walk, which records a result on every active candidate since each keeps at least one link (`D12-ingest`, `D16-enrichers`), re-projects every facts row (category evidence decision 12, `D06-passes`). After a rollback the previous release's own re-projection of a candidate writes the union again, which is accepted (category evidence decision 11): `ClassifiedBy` reads such a mixed set as `links`, since not every category in it carries `brief`, and `RecordsDiffer` of it and the records false, until this release next writes the row.
- **The records are read, not kept** (category evidence decision 4): `Facts.Records` is answered by `GetFacts` from the candidate's newest `link_facts` result at the read, sorted as `Categories` is, and the facts table gains no column for it, so the previous release runs on the schema unchanged and a row it wrote still reads its records. No write takes a `Facts`, so nothing writes the field. `Records` follows `Categories` in `store.Facts`, the two category lists side by side, as the candidate page shows the records beside the categories that decide.
- **`Classified` follows `Matching`** in `store.ProspectListRead`, as the prospect list page shows the count beside matching (category evidence decision 5). It counts the answered members, not the matching candidates, as the decision's "how many of the answered members" says, and reads each member's facts' categories, its facts row's at the read, so the count and each member's classification agree.
- **A facts row exists only beside a result**: `FillFacts` and every write build a row only for an active candidate holding a result; a candidate with none has no row and reads every fact unknown, and a merge deletes the merged candidate's row, one row per active candidate (enrichers decision 5). The row is keyed by the candidate and has no other index: the prospect list query reaches it by key from each candidate, as it reached the stored judgments, which have no index of their own but their key either, so the primary key is all of the "indexes" the decisions ask `0012` to create for the facts.
- **The derived kinds run in the store** (decided for this delivery; enrichers decisions 9 and 15): `Derive` and a ruling's re-run call the pure functions of `internal/enrichers` inside the store's write, so a merge's moves, its fresh derived results and its re-judge are one write, as the judge already is; the store imports `enrichers`, which imports nothing of the store or the engine. A merge or a split records a fresh result of each derived kind whose inputs are not empty for each touched active candidate, whether or not that candidate is due for the kind (R-R7HY-562T, `D12-ingest`), so the survivor's facts reflect the moved links even where the moved results' order would leave an older one newest.
- **Newest is recorded later, not stamped later** (decided 2026-10-15): two results of one kind on a candidate are ordered by when they were recorded, which is deterministic where two share a second, and survives a merge that moves the other candidate's results among the survivor's, as the rulings are ordered. A survivor whose merged candidate's last check came after its own takes that result as its newest.
- **The due set is a keyset page in id order** (website log P8, P12, enrichers decision 3, P17): `DueEnrichments` examines at most `n` active candidates after a cursor id and answers the due ones among them, each with its inputs, and the last examined id as the next cursor, so a pass holds one page and a candidate already answered is never answered again in the same pass, whatever its results become; due is a comparison of inputs, never of ages, so `live`, `none` and their arithmetic are gone with the refresh ages. A candidate whose id falls after the cursor may still be reached in the pass that is running, since ids are random. The window's statement is not exported and its plan is not required: the due set is the pass's alone, read by no page or tool, and `D06-passes` and `D15-website-check` bound what the pass holds. The inputs of `link_facts` are computed in Go from the candidate's links, so the due window reads each candidate of the window and its newest result of the kind by key; which statements the build chooses is its own. `Derive`, `JudgeAll` and `FillFacts` walk the active candidates the same way. Before P17 the call read on until it had found `n` due candidates, so a pass with few due read the whole space in one read transaction; a page may now answer none beside a cursor that is not empty, and the pass goes on until the cursor is empty.
- **The units and their bounds** (P16, P17, P18, `shape/`'s Bounded work): disk and wall time may grow with the space, memory and the work of one call may not. Each unit is one call, one transaction and one page: an `Attach` holds its batch of `passes.BatchSize` observations and, per candidate it touches, that candidate's own links, folded as read; `MarkGone`, `JudgeAll`, `Derive`, `FillFacts`, `PrunePasses`, `DueEnrichments` and `ActiveAddressKeys` one page of the `n` their caller passes, `passes.BatchSize` from every caller; a merge or a split the candidates it names or creates and their own rows; `DeriveAmong` and `DueAmong` the ids they are given, a prospect list's members, at most `ProspectListSizeMax`, or one candidate; `ReplaceZipDrive` the release's ZIP table, configuration; `ApplyParks` one derivation's plan and `PriorParks` every park held, each bounded by the space's industrial parcels, not by the parcel rows read. A read bounded by one key is one query: one candidate's own links, rulings, enrichments or pages, the largest on the `us` space holding 3,224 links, which is what a subject or a display re-derivation of that candidate reads. Only the observable side is required, what a call changes or answers beyond its page or its ids (R-EN4W-HQ09, R-Y3SS-OY52) and the memory of the paged calls, the upkeep and the projection (R-5LEB-MYTX, R-R5M9-468R, R-LL7Q-0WU0, R-Y053-JMWZ): a page of 10 over 100,000 candidates allocates within 1 MiB, which a read of the 100,000 ids alone exceeds, so a call that reads the whole set and cuts it to its page fails; that a statement spans no more than its page cannot be observed by use, as no test counts statements or reads tables.
- **A prospect list's passing set is read whole** (a departure from `shape/`'s Bounded work): an open records the list's passing set, every matching candidate's id in rank order before the size cut, as one JSON value in its `opens` row (basis decision 9), and a read compares against it, so `ReadProspectList` and `OpenProspectList` hold every passing id of the list at once, proportional to the matching count, not to the page. Paging it would need the set kept as rows, a schema change P16 leaves out of this release, whose previous release must run on the same schema. It grows with the matching count, which the owner's filters bound and an unfiltered list makes every active candidate; the two lists the decisions log measured on `us` matched 34 and 117. A later release that keeps the passing set as rows can page it.
- **The mark is an opaque sequence** (decided 2026-10-15): `EnrichmentMark` answers the highest `seq` held and `EnrichmentAfter` the last result above it of one kind and inputs, on `enrichments_inputs`, which `0012` adds now that the reuse matches inputs rather than the source (for a website result the two are the same domain); results are never removed and a merge keeps their sequence, so a mark never reaches a result recorded after it. The value is not contract: only that results recorded before it are not found and results recorded after it are (R-GX77-7X5D).
- **`derived` sits after `unranged`** in `store.Pass` and `store.Counts`, as everywhere the counts are listed (`D06-passes`), since the pass page lists it first among an enrich's counts. `Runs`, `RunsFailed`, `Pages` and `Cost` follow `None`, `Target`, `EnrichKind` and `Scraper` follow `Trigger`, in the order the pass page, the trail and the tools list them (`D08-pages`, `D07-events`, `D06-passes`); `Cost` is an integer of nano-dollars, as prompts reports a run's cost, so the pass's sum is exact (site text decision 14). `Brief` follows `Minutes` in `store.Facts`, the last fact before `Written`, and `SiteText` in `Ranked`, as the prospect list page lists a member's brief time after its site text time (`D08-pages`, brief decisions 8 and 12). `WebsiteSource` follows `WebsiteURL` in `store.Facts`, the source beside the site it names, as the tools answer `website_source` after `website_url` (`D10-tools`). `MinutesSource`, `Miles` and `RouteAddress` follow `Minutes`, the source beside the minutes it names and the route's other facts after it, as the tools answer them (`D10-tools`); `Miles` is a float, one decimal as the result found it, and nil when the ZIP decided.
- **A campaign use keeps the earliest add, the first rejection and the latest delivery, and every write is safe to repeat** (campaigns decisions 16 and 17): the bus retries a delivery until it is answered and does not order events, so a write must leave the same use whatever order and however often the events come. The earliest add is when the business was first used, which is what the filter measures (campaigns decision 18); the first rejection is the one campaigns made final (campaigns decision 5); the latest delivery is "last delivered". An add's prospect list travels with its time, so the prospect list shown is the one the business came from when it was first added. Comparing at the stored second makes equal times leave the use alone.
- **Writes follow the merge pointer; the read does not** (campaigns log D1): campaigns keeps the candidate id it was given, which a later merge may retire, so each write records on the active end of the chain, as the history belongs to the business as it stands. `CampaignUses` answers the id it is given, as `Links`, `Enrichments` and `Pages` do, so the read of one candidate never borrows another's rows; the survivor already holds what a merge moved (`D12-ingest`), and the tool and the page show nothing on a merged candidate.
- **An empty candidate or campaign is `ErrInvalid`, a zero time `ErrUnreachable`** (decided for this delivery): the ids come from another app's event, so a missing one is a refusal, though the delivery skips such an event before it calls (`D06-passes`), whereas every bus event carries its time, so a zero time is a value no consumer sends, as everywhere in this store. An unknown candidate is `ErrNotFound`, which the delivery also skips: a business campaigns names that candidates never held has no history to keep.
- **The effective time is defined here, once** (campaigns decision 18): the filter of `D14-prospect-lists` and any reader of a use measure from the same moment, the add, or, while the add is not yet known, the earlier of the rejection and the delivery, so a use told out of order still counts from the first sign of it.
- **`MinCampaignAgeDays` follows `FilingUnknown`** in `store.ProspectListFilters`, beside the other age filter, `omitempty` as every member (log 16), so a prospect list without it stores and answers the same JSON as before and a pointer to 0 is kept, since `omitempty` leaves out only a nil pointer. The stored filters gain a member and lose none, an expand the previous release reads by ignoring it (Known limits). What it admits and which values `ValidProspectListFilters` refuses are `D14-prospect-lists`'.
- **The search's cases ride on its found value, with no migration** (empty sites decision 4): `reason`, `website`, `website_from`, `site_text` and `case` are added members of the `website_search` result's `found` JSON (`D16-enrichers`), which the website decision reads as it reads the confirmation. Investigated against release-71 (`8b761c11`): it calls `WebsiteSearch.Valid`, which refuses unknown members, only on the write side, in `AddEnrichments` and on its own prompt run's result; every read, the facts rebuild, `FillFacts`, `GetFacts`, the subject's website facts, `Enrichments`, the basis and kinds reads, the prospect list read, the `candidate` tool, which passes `found` through whole, the candidate page and the judge, decodes `found` without refusing unknown members, and a scratch test against that release served the page, answered the tool and projected the facts as expected; it decides due from the `inputs` column alone. Columns for the case or the website were rejected: they would need DDL and a backfill to say what the found value already says, and the projection already decodes `found`; the cost of the choice is the rollback's re-run (Known limits).
- **`0025` is a plain expand** (empty sites decision 5; the root `AGENTS.md`'s rule holds): three `passes` columns, `search_different`, `search_stands` and `search_none`, each `INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, so a pass recorded before reads 0 of each case and release-71's inserts, updates and prunes, which name only its own columns, run on it unchanged (R-WIIB-6TNQ, R-WM60-C4VT, R-WOLT-3OD7). `SearchDifferent`, `SearchStands` and `SearchNone` follow `NewCandidates` in `store.Pass` and `store.Counts`, appended after the walk's counts as the pass record lists them (`D06-passes`), so no field moves; what each counts is `D06-passes`'.
- **The Places URL is a store read, not a candidate field** (`PlacesURL`): it is read from the place records at each call, never written by `D12-ingest`, so it cannot lag a walk. Rejected: a `Candidate` member, which would be one more display value every link write must keep current and would re-shape the candidate for a value only the website reads.
- **A confirmed search ranks above an unchecked Places URL** (the decisions document's items 3 and 4, confirmed by the coordinator): a site a search found and a page confirmed is a site that answered, while a Places URL not yet given to the check is only a claim; so an unchecked Places URL ranks below a confirmed search and above a search that found none (R-QJ7S-Y7FR), and the next enrich gives it to the check. A search that found nothing searched without the site Places reports, so its `none` decides only once the check has been given that URL.
- **The other values are stored, not computed at the read** (facts from the candidate): `D12-ingest` writes the pick's other names, addresses, phones and emails into the candidate's row in every write that writes the display, a write that changes its links, the rebuild, and `AddEnrichments` of a `drive_route` result and `FillFacts` here (R-HTD3-0YUG, R-HUKZ-EQL5), so `GetCandidate`, a prospect list's members, the card and the `candidate` tool read one row and compute nothing, as every other store read; the row is rebuilt by a full ingest whenever the orders change. Rejected: a read that picks again from the links at every `GetCandidate`, which would need the routed address's rule at the read and would answer a value no write ever recorded.
- **A recorded route rewrites the display in its own write, and every start rewrites each routed candidate's** (decision 18, facts from the candidate): `AddEnrichments` rewrites the display of each candidate it records a `drive_route` result on, the only kind the display reads, so a route shows when it is recorded, not at the next link write or full ingest; a result of any other kind leaves the candidate's row alone, so an enrich pass is no partial rebuild. "After an origin change" is read as every start: the store keeps no record of the origin it last ran at, `FillFacts` already re-projects the facts row of every candidate holding a `drive_route` result at the store's origin at each start, and only such a candidate's display can depend on the origin, so `FillFacts` rewrites those candidates' displays beside their rows, and at a start whose origin did not change the rewrite changes nothing and `updated` stays. Rejected: rewriting every candidate of the page at each start, which, though the fill now runs after `READY=1`, reads every candidate's links and writes every display at every start, lengthening the time passes are refused `busy` and making each start's fill a partial rebuild; recording the last origin so as to rewrite only after a change, a column for no difference anyone sees. The rewrite reads the candidate's links and its standing route by key, so both calls keep their bounds (R-Y053-JMWZ, R-5LEB-MYTX), and it lands in the call's one write, so the requirements that make concurrent calls behave as one after another and a busy write retried or abandoned whole cover it unchanged (R-MUPS-FJX3, R-5BN4-KSWD, R-DS8O-A9S0). The route moves only the address and the facts row reads no address field, so the row is what it would be without the rewrite; every kind's inputs are computed from the candidate at each read, so a routed address that moves the display's street or ZIP makes `website_search` or `drive_minutes` due at the next enrich with nothing written here, and `drive_route` itself reads the record-chosen address and is not made due by its own result.
- **People keep their column and become sourced values**: `Candidate.People` is `[]sources.Sourced[sources.Person]`, the pick's own shape, and the column keeps the previous release's objects with `Source` and `Key` added beside `Name` and `Role`, so that release reads them unchanged. Rejected: a store `Person` type repeating `sources.Person`'s fields, and a parallel slice of sources, either of which would carry the same values in a second shape.
- **Each chosen fact keeps its record key beside its source** (`NameRecord`, `AddressRecord`, `PhoneRecord`, `EmailRecord`): an enricher records the record of each input it read (`D16-enrichers`), and the subject is built from this row, so the key is kept where the value is.
- **Known since and newest filing come from `link_facts`**: the facts row stays the projection of the newest result of each kind, so the two dates and their sources are what the newest `link_facts` result found, by the orders (`D16-enrichers`), and a forced `link_facts` in a full ingest rebuilds them. Rejected: picking them at the facts row's write from the links, which would make the row a function of the links between results.
- **A Places URL is unchecked by its presence in a check's inputs** (`website.URLs`, `D15-website-check`), not by the newest check alone: a check given the URL that found nothing still leaves it checked, and a candidate whose newest check was of its email domain alone, recorded before a walk found its website, reads `places` until the check is given it.
- **The Places URL is the first places link's offering a usable website, not strictly the newest place record's** (decided for this change; R-RYYW-L4WX): each link's place record is the newest walk's that found its place, so a later walk that saw no website empties that link's, but among a candidate's places links the URL comes from the first, in the order `Links` answers, whose place record's website is an offered URL, so a link with no website, or one the check cannot be given, does not hide one a later link offers. Rejected: the newest place record across all the candidate's places links, a second order beside the one `Links` already answers with its ties settled, which would let a newer record with nothing to check hide an older one's site.

## Consumer tasks

The engine (`D06-passes`), `c` the caller, records an `ingest` call's pass with `p, err := st.AddPass(ctx, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindIngest, Origin: store.OriginIngest, Trigger: store.TriggerIngest, Scraper: scraper, UserID: c.UserID, UserEmail: c.Email, RequestID: c.RequestID})`, `scraper` empty for a full ingest, and a `judge` call's with `st.AddPass(ctx, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindJudge, Origin: store.OriginJudge, Trigger: store.TriggerJudgeTool, UserID: c.UserID, UserEmail: c.Email, RequestID: c.RequestID})`, `store.ErrBusy` answering `busy`; an ingest delivery with `st.AddPass(ctx, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindIngest, Origin: store.OriginRun, Trigger: run, UserID: user, RequestID: c.RequestID, Delivery: d.Event.ID})` and a judge delivery with `st.AddPass(ctx, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindJudge, Origin: store.OriginCron, Trigger: store.TriggerJudge, UserID: user, RequestID: c.RequestID, Delivery: d.Event.ID})`, `store.ErrDelivered` answering `Skip` and `store.ErrBusy` `Fail`. An ingest hands each batch to `n, touched, err := st.Attach(ctx, run, force, batch)`, then marks the absent rows page by page with `next, g, err := st.MarkGone(ctx, scraper, run, after, passes.BatchSize)`, `after` starting empty and then the `next` the call before answered, until `next` is empty, adds the `store.Counts` they answer and ends with `st.FinishPass(ctx, p.ID, total)`; a judge reads `at`, the pass's judge time, once from its clock, judges page by page with `next, n, err := st.JudgeAll(ctx, after, passes.BatchSize, at)` the same way and ends with `st.FinishPass(ctx, p.ID, total)`, `total` the sum of each `n`; a full ingest does an ingest's writes with `force` true for each scraper's current run, then the forced walks, then the judge's pages, and finishes with the sum; a scraper ingest does an ingest's writes for that scraper's current run alone. A pass whose work failed or was interrupted ends with `st.FailPass(ctx, p.ID, store.ReasonScrapes)` or its other reason. An ingest delivery asks `_, err := st.FindDelivered(ctx, d.Event.ID, run)` before it reads scrapes and a judge delivery `st.FindDelivered(ctx, d.Event.ID, "")`, a nil error answering `Skip` and `store.ErrNotFound` going on. The `enrich` tool's start records `st.AddPass(ctx, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindEnrich, Origin: store.OriginEnrich, Trigger: store.TriggerEnrich, UserID: c.UserID, UserEmail: c.Email, RequestID: c.RequestID})`, and an ingest that ends `done` its follow-on with `st.AddPass(ctx, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindEnrich, Origin: store.OriginPass, Trigger: p.ID, RequestID: p.RequestID})`, an enrich that wrote anything a judge the same way with `store.KindJudge`, `store.ErrBusy` answering `busy` or starting nothing. A targeted enrich records `st.AddPass(ctx, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindEnrichTarget, Origin: store.OriginEnrich, Trigger: store.TriggerEnrich, Target: target, EnrichKind: kind, UserID: c.UserID, UserEmail: c.Email, RequestID: c.RequestID})`, `store.ErrBusy` answering `busy`, and walks `ids`, its prospect list's members' ids in order or its one candidate's: a derived kind with `n, err := st.DeriveAmong(ctx, kind, ids, force)`, a fetched or prompts kind with `ds, err := st.DueAmong(ctx, kind, ids, force)`; for each company of a prompts kind `s, err := st.Subject(ctx, d.Candidate)` gives the run's input, `s.Pages` and `s.Facts` among it for `brief` and `s.Street`, `s.Phone`, `s.Check`, `s.Facts`, `s.Website`, `s.WebsiteSource` and `s.SiteText` among it for `website_search`, and, for `site_text` alone, `pc, err := st.CountPages(ctx, d.Candidate)` before the run and after it gives the result's `pc.URLs` and the copies the run added; it ends with `st.FinishPass(ctx, p.ID, store.Counts{Due: due, Runs: runs, RunsFailed: failed, Pages: pages, Cost: cost})`, for `website_search` with `SearchDifferent`, `SearchStands` and `SearchNone` beside them, the results it recorded whose found value's `Case` is each case word (empty sites decision 5), or `st.FailPass(ctx, p.ID, store.ReasonPrompts)`. The enrich work (`D06-passes`) runs each derived kind first, `next, n, err := st.Derive(ctx, enrichers.KindLinkFacts, after, false, passes.BatchSize)` window by window, `after` the `next` the call before answered, until `next` is empty, adding each `n` to `Derived`; a full ingest does the same with `force` true between its ingest and its judge. A fetched kind (`D15-website-check`) reads `mark, err := st.EnrichmentMark(ctx)` when it begins, then `next, due, err := st.DueEnrichments(ctx, sources.WebsiteCheck, after, passes.BatchSize)` page by page, `after` the `next` the call before answered, until `next` is empty, a page that answers no `due` going on to the next, asks `e, err := st.EnrichmentAfter(ctx, sources.WebsiteCheck, d.Inputs, mark)` for each inputs, `store.ErrNotFound` meaning not yet fetched in this pass, records with `rs, err := st.AddEnrichments(ctx, []store.Enrichment{{Candidate: id, Kind: sources.WebsiteCheck, Inputs: in, Source: r.Source, Found: r.Found, Cost: r.Cost}})`, decodes each `x.Found` into a `website.Result` to count its state, and ends with `st.FinishPass(ctx, p.ID, store.Counts{Derived: derived, Due: due, Fetched: fetched, HTTPS: https, HTTP: http, None: none})`. A judge reads the facts row through the store's own judge path (`D13-judging`). The start calls `st.ReplaceZipDrive(ctx, candidates.ZipDrive())`, then `next, err := st.FillFacts(ctx, after, passes.BatchSize)` page by page until `next` is empty, then `st.RunningPasses(ctx)` (`D03-serve`); the start and the sweep `k, err := st.PrunePasses(ctx, s.KeepPasses, passes.BatchSize)` until `k` is 0. `decide`, `exclude` and `include` re-judge through `st.Decide` (`D12-ingest`, `D13-judging`).

The parks pass (`D06-passes`) records a delivery's with `st.AddPass(ctx, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindParks, Origin: store.OriginRun, Trigger: run, UserID: user, RequestID: c.RequestID, Delivery: d.Event.ID})`, the start's with `st.AddPass(ctx, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindParks, Origin: store.OriginStart, Trigger: run, RequestID: requestID})` once `st.ParksRun(ctx)` answers another run, and an `edit_park_rules` call's with `p, err := st.SetParkRules(ctx, e, store.Pass{Kind: store.KindParks, Origin: store.OriginRules, Trigger: store.TriggerParkRules, UserID: c.UserID, UserEmail: c.Email, RequestID: c.RequestID})`, `store.ErrInvalid` answering `invalid` and `store.ErrBusy` `busy`. Its work reads `rules, _, err := st.ParkRules(ctx)`, the keys with `keys, after, err := st.ActiveAddressKeys(ctx, after, passes.BatchSize)` until `after` is empty, and `prior, err := st.PriorParks(ctx)`, and ends with `p, err = st.ApplyParks(ctx, p.ID, run, counties, plan, counts)`, or `st.FailPass` with its reason. The `parks` tool and the parks page read `st.ListParks(ctx, store.ParkFilter{County: county, Walked: walked, Gone: gone}, sort, key, id, n)`, `ListParksReversed` for Prev and Last, `st.GetPark(ctx, id)`, `st.ParkParcels(ctx, id)`, `st.CountParks(ctx)`, `st.ParkCountiesHeld(ctx)`, `st.ParkCountiesRead(ctx)` and `st.ParksRun(ctx)`; `park_rules` reads `st.ParkRules(ctx)`. A test applies a plan it builds with `parks.Match` and reads it back, and opens a database with the first twenty-two files to prove `0023` an expand.

The prospect list tools (`D10-tools`), `c` the caller, check `store.ValidProspectListName`, `store.ValidProspectListSize`, `store.ValidProspectListFilters` and `store.ValidProspectListOrder` on what was passed and `store.ValidProspectListID` on the id before the store, on the id `store.MapListID` gives (`coh_` ids of the previous release mapped to `pl_`), then `create_prospect_list` calls `k, err := st.CreateProspectList(ctx, store.ProspectList{Name: a.Name, Size: size, Filters: f, Order: o, CreatedUserID: c.UserID, CreatedUserEmail: c.Email})`, `size` being `store.ProspectListSizeDefault` when none was passed and `f` and `o` built from the arguments with `store.OrderRevenueLow`, `store.DirectionAsc` and the like as their words; `edit_prospect_list` `k, err := st.EditProspectList(ctx, store.ProspectListEdit{ID: store.MapListID(a.ProspectList), Name: a.Name, Size: sz, Filters: fp, Order: op, UserID: c.UserID, UserEmail: c.Email})`, each pointer nil when its argument was absent, `fp` `&store.ProspectListFilters{}` for a passed `{}` and `op` `&[]store.ProspectListOrder{}` for a passed `[]`; `delete_prospect_list` `k, err := st.DeleteProspectList(ctx, store.MapListID(a.ProspectList))`; `prospect_list` `r, err := st.OpenProspectList(ctx, store.MapListID(id), c.UserID, c.Email)`, and with `peek` true, as the legacy `cohort` tool beside it, `r, err := st.ReadProspectList(ctx, store.MapListID(id))`, answering `r.ProspectList`, `r.Matching`, `r.Classified`, `r.ByBasis`, `r.Changes` (its `Since`, its `DefinitionChanged` among `store.DefinitionFilters`, `store.DefinitionOrder` and `store.DefinitionSize`, and each `Joined` and `Left` with its `Why` among `store.WhyNew` to `store.WhyGone`), each `m` of `r.Members` from `m.Candidate`, `m.Judgments` (`m.Judgments.Operating` and `m.Judgments.Excluded` among them) and `m.Facts` (`m.Facts.FleetVerdict` and `m.Facts.WebsiteSource` among them, and `store.ClassifiedBy(m.Facts.Categories)` as its classification), `m.SiteText`, `m.Brief` and `m.Basis` and `r.Rejects`; `prospect_lists` `ks, err := st.ListProspectLists(ctx, name, id, n+1)` after `paging.Token{Tool: "prospect_lists"}.Decode(page, &name, &id)`, the `n`th prospect list's `k.Name` and `k.ID` giving `next`; `reject` and `unreject` `x, err := st.Reject(ctx, store.Reject{Kind: store.KindReject, ProspectList: store.MapListID(a.ProspectList), Candidate: a.Candidate, Why: a.Why, UserID: c.UserID, UserEmail: c.Email})`, with `store.KindUnreject` for `unreject`, answering `x`, whose `Why`, `UserID`, `UserEmail` and `At` are empty for an unreject of a pair with no standing reject; and `candidate` adds `j, err := st.GetJudgments(ctx, id)`, answered as its `judgments`, `xs, err := st.Rejects(ctx, id)`, `ps, err := st.Pages(ctx, id)`, `es, err := st.Enrichments(ctx, id)`, answered as its `enrichment_results` and giving its brief, its `basis` as `store.BasisOf(es)` and its `kinds` as `store.KindsOf(es)`, and `f, err := st.GetFacts(ctx, id)`, answered as its `enrichments`' `facts`, each fact with where it came from: `f.Categories` with their sources, `f.WebsiteSource` and `f.Written`, beside `store.ClassifiedBy(f.Categories)`, `store.RecordsDiffer(f.Categories, f.Records)` and, when that is true, `f.Records` as what the records say; `store.ErrNotFound` from `GetFacts` giving no `facts`. `add_page`, after its own checks, which read `st.GetFacts`, `st.CountPages` and `st.Pages`, records `pg, err := st.AddPage(ctx, store.Page{Candidate: a.Candidate, URL: u, Title: w.Title, Text: w.Text, Size: w.Size, UserID: c.UserID, UserEmail: c.Email})`, `u` the URL it keeps and `w` what it fetched (`D10-tools`), `store.ErrPageCap` answering its cap refusal and `store.ErrInvalid` its no-website refusal. `store.ErrNotFound` answers `not_found`, `store.ErrInvalid` `invalid`, and any error matching `store.ErrUnreachable` `unavailable`.
